# Daily Dump

Treat chat as the capture surface and `/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/inbox/YYYY-MM-DD.md` as the live readable note.

## Capture Workflow

1. Read `/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/AGENTS.md` and `/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/inbox/AGENTS.md`.
2. Resolve the capture date and time in `Asia/Manila`. Use today unless the user clearly identifies another date.
3. Open today's note. If it does not exist, create it from `assets/daily-note-template.md`, replacing the placeholders.
4. Interpret the new dump in the context of the existing note:
   - Add concrete commitments or follow-ups to `Today` as unchecked tasks.
   - When later evidence clearly completes a task, change it to `[x]`, strike through its text, and append a timestamped completion annotation.
   - Preserve cancelled or superseded tasks as completed, struck-through entries with the reason.
   - Mark blocked or waiting work without completing it.
   - Add observations, ideas, decisions, feelings, and non-actionable context to `Notes`.
   - Update an existing entry instead of creating a semantic duplicate.
5. Append the user's raw dump once under `Timeline`, using a timestamped subheading and Markdown blockquote. Preserve wording; do not silently correct names or claims.
6. Update `Last Updated` without changing the capture date.
7. Use `apply_patch` for edits. Do not run a full ingest, update wiki pages, edit `actions.md`, commit, or push unless the user explicitly asks.
8. Reply briefly with what changed and a clickable link to the daily note.

## State Rules

- Treat inferred daily tasks as provisional until ingest promotes unresolved work to `actions.md`.
- Infer routine state transitions when the evidence is clear. Ask only when marking the wrong task would materially distort the record.
- Use `UNCERTAIN:` for ambiguous owners, names, acronyms, dates, or high-impact interpretations.
- Never remove raw timeline entries.
- Never erase an earlier task merely because its state changed.
- Keep annotations short and chronological.
- If a dump contains both an update and a thought, update `Today`, add the thought to `Notes`, and preserve the full raw message in `Timeline`.

## Invocation Boundary

Explicit `$dump` invocation always activates this workflow. Implicit activation requires a clear capture signal such as `dump:` or an unambiguous request to log or remember something. A bare statement in an unrelated task is not enough evidence that the user wants it written to the Mind Palace.

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T12:07:50.591Z

First, confirm Prod Airflow has synced commit `6ddeec1` and shows `japan_occto_operating_capacity_backfill_dag`. Don’t trigger it yet.

Then run this in Prod Grafana Explore:

```sql
WITH daily AS (
  SELECT
    target_date,
    COUNT(*) AS total_rows,
    COUNT_IF(effective_operating_capacity_mw IS NULL) AS operating_nulls
  FROM japan_interconnector_effective_capacity_all
  GROUP BY target_date
)
SELECT
  MIN(target_date) AS repair_start_date,
  MAX(target_date) AS repair_end_date,
  COUNT(*) AS affected_dates,
  MIN(total_rows) AS min_rows_per_date,
  MAX(total_rows) AS max_rows_per_date
FROM daily
WHERE operating_nulls > 0;
```

Paste the five results. I’ll use those to give you the exact one-run Prod JSON—we shouldn’t assume QA’s end date.

### User - 2026-07-30T12:15:17.298Z

backfill dag running as we speak. 

Why can't I trigger the yearly->daily alongside backfill?

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T12:15:32.842Z

Because they mutate the same source inventory and effective publication that the backfill is validating and eventually replacing.

While the mapped preparation tasks are read-only, the final backfill task will:

- Recheck source fingerprints
- Snapshot the current effective baseline
- Replace historical source objects
- Publish affected-year files and the shared publication manifest

Meanwhile, yearly→daily DAGs would also upload capacity sources and trigger reconciliation against that same manifest. Cross-DAG runs are not protected by the backfill’s `max_active_runs=1`.

Best case, the backfill detects the concurrent change and fails safely. Worse, two reconciliations race and the last publisher preserves an outdated view of the other run’s changes.

So let the backfill finish first, then run yearly → monthly → weekly → daily sequentially. That also preserves intended precedence: broad yearly forecasts first, most specific daily values last.

### User - 2026-07-29T16:34:24.589Z

pleasantu srurpised to see u fixed replace smp core prod -> qrm blabla. I did not even think of that, yet you saw it. Thnks. What was the wisdom behind that?

### Assistant - 2026-07-29T16:34:41.190Z

The guiding principle was: **follow the artifact’s verified provenance, not the legacy configuration name.**

`0.6.0` was demonstrably published to:

```text
qrm-dms-core-pypi-dev
```

But `smp-tool` still attempted to install `smp-common` from:

```text
smp-core-pypi-prod
```

A version bump alone would therefore produce an image definition that asked the wrong repository for `0.6.0`. It might look correct in `pyproject.toml`, but tomorrow’s Docker build would fail—or worse, resolve an unintended package later.

Changing the index also required following the whole dependency chain:

```text
pyproject index
→ uv.lock artifact URLs
→ uv authentication variable names
→ Docker BuildKit secret IDs
→ local build scripts
→ manual GitHub workflow inputs
```

Leaving any one of those on the old `SMP_CORE_PROD` naming would create a subtle authentication failure.

A few things made the decision high-confidence:

- The `0.6.0` publication workflow verified the QRM-DMS repository through read-back.
- The exact wheel and sdist hashes were available.
- Japan’s working `0.6.0` lock independently matched those hashes.
- Existing GitHub secret values could be mapped into the new environment-variable names, avoiding an immediate secret-provisioning change.
- `smp-tool` owns the image dependency, so this was the correct place to fix the source. Git-sync remained untouched.

In short: the old name reflected historical package plumbing; the new configuration reflects where the package actually lives. The useful instinct was to trace the dependency end-to-end instead of treating the version string as the whole change.

# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/repos/smp

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# AGENTS.md

This file provides guidance to AI coding agents working under `/Users/qn5792/repos/smp`.

## Overview

`/smp` is a parent directory, not a monorepo. It contains separate repositories for the **SMP (Scraper Management Platform)** and adjacent APAC scraper work. Read the repo-local `AGENTS.md` before changing files inside any child repo.

## Current Repos

| Repo | Purpose | Tooling |
|---|---|---|
| `scraper-scripts` | Raw standalone scraper scripts and behavioral references before Airflow adaptation | Python 3.13, `uv` |
| `smp-india` | India-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-japan` | Japan-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-tool` | Airflow Docker image, Helm values, Kubernetes scripts, Okta auth, Minikube/EKS setup | Python 3.12, `uv`, Helm, Kubernetes |
| `smp-dashboard` | Grafana dashboards and `cdh-dataset-manager` for CommonDataHub datasets | Python 3.11, `uv` |
| `apac-tsdb-scraper` | Poetry/solaris-sfw TSDB scrapers deployed through the older Lambda/tag flow | Python 3.12, Poetry |

Legacy names still appear in older docs and code comments:

- `scraper-dags` is now split into `smp-india` and `smp-japan`.
- `scraper-tool` is now `smp-tool`.
- `scraper-dashboard` is now `smp-dashboard`.

## End-to-End SMP Flow

```text
scraper-scripts  ->  smp-india / smp-japan  ->  smp-tool  ->  smp-dashboard
(reference impl)     (Airflow DAGs)             (runtime)     (CDH + Grafana)
```

Use `scraper-scripts` as the behavioral source when porting or validating a DAG. Region-specific DAG implementation now belongs in either `smp-india` or `smp-japan`; shared Airflow/platform behavior should come from `smp-common` or `smp-tool`, not be copied between region repos.

## Shared Rules

- Do not commit credentials, tokens, kubeconfigs, generated local secrets, or one-off path rewrites.
- Private dependencies come from ENGIE Artifactory. Prefer fixing local credentials over changing package indexes.
- Keep region boundaries explicit: India DAG code in `smp-india`, Japan DAG code in `smp-japan`.
- For Airflow DAGs, keep heavy imports out of module scope because Airflow parses DAG files frequently.
- For dashboard work, verify the upstream parquet/S3/CDH shape before changing Grafana JSON or dataset definitions.
- For infra changes, call out whether the change affects image build, Helm values, auth, DAG sync, runtime secrets, or Kubernetes namespaces.

## Cross-Repo Workflow: Porting A Script To A DAG

1. Run and observe the source script in `scraper-scripts`; capture inputs, outputs, credentials, file names, date logic, and failure behavior.
2. Choose the DAG repo by market ownership: `smp-india` or `smp-japan`.
3. Check whether an existing DAG for the same source should be extended before creating a new DAG.
4. Implement under `dags/src/`, keeping task runtime scraper modules under `dags/src/scripts/`.
5. Validate locally with `ruff`, `pytest`, and, when practical, `airflow dags test`.
6. Coordinate deployment through the appropriate branch/environment and `smp-tool` git-sync/Helm configuration.
7. Update `smp-dashboard` only after the DAG output is stable and visible where CDH/Grafana expects it.

## Branch And Environment Notes

- GitHub repos commonly use `main`; some DAG deployment flows still reference `dev`, `qa`, `master`, or region-specific branches.
- `smp-tool` has region-aware config under `config/japan/<env>/` and `config/india/<env>/`.
- Airflow namespaces are region-specific in current scripts, for example `smp-dev`, `smp-qa`, `smp-prod`, `smp-india-dev`, `smp-india-qa`, and `smp-india-prod`.
- Treat any old `scraper-dags`/ADO git-sync reference as potentially stale and verify against current `smp-tool` config before editing deployment behavior.

# FY2025 OCCTO TSDB Backfill

## Summary

Create `agent/occto-tsdb-fy2025-backfill` directly from `origin/agent/occto-smp-common-tsdb-poc` at `1d436b01c973f4db5afeaa39d0286525a09a9454`.

Add a reusable, manually triggered Japan DAG that publishes validated parquet to all 35 TSDB series. Its default range is FY2025:

- Start: `2025-04-01` JST
- End: `2026-03-31` JST, inclusive
- Capacity: 28 half-hourly forecast series
- Actual flow: 7 five-minute actual series
- Expected complete total: 1,226,400 points

No `smp-common` change or release is anticipated.

## Implementation

- Add `japan_occto_tsdb_backfill_dag` with:
  - `start_date`, default `2025-04-01`
  - `end_date`, default `2026-03-31`
  - `write`, default `false`
  - Endpoint selected automatically by deployment: UAT on dev/QA, production on prod.
  - Historical, inclusive range validation with a maximum of 366 days.
- Read only durable validated data:
  - Capacity from the current effective-capacity publication manifest and its verified parquet snapshot.
  - Actual flow from datasource parquet, requiring exactly one unambiguous artifact per date.
  - Do not scrape OCCTO, reconcile capacity, mutate datasource S3, or provision catalogue entries.
- Preflight and publish bounded chunks:
  - Capacity in at most 31-day chunks.
  - Actual flow in seven-day chunks.
  - Validate exact timestamp grids, series sets, finite values, counts, and duplicates per chunk.
  - A source-incomplete chunk is skipped and reported; other complete chunks continue.
  - Process capacity followed by actual flow, strictly one chunk at a time.
- Extend Japan’s capacity publisher with an optional exclusive application end so a chunk cannot include later effective data.
- Add an actual-flow parquet publication adapter; keep publication mapping independent from the consistency-audit mapping.
- Publish each chunk through `smp-common==0.6.0`:
  - Read and diff before every write.
  - Use the effective-capacity manifest’s `reconciled_at_utc` as the forecast version.
  - Preserve exact seven/28-series coverage.
  - Verify changed points immediately after writing.
  - Stop immediately on stale forecast versions, source mutation, SDK errors, or read-back failure.
- Persist a JSON-safe campaign report containing source identity, endpoint, range, chunk statuses, gaps, unchanged/written rows, verification results, and totals.
  - Missing-source chunks produce `completed-with-warnings` and a successful Airflow run.
  - Publication or verification errors fail the run.
  - Rerunning after repairing parquet is safe: completed points resolve to `no-change`.

## TDD and Validation

- Begin with failing tests for the missing DAG and backfill orchestration contract.
- Cover:
  - FY2025 defaults and stage-derived endpoint.
  - Range validation and deterministic chunk boundaries.
  - Exact capacity and actual-flow grids/counts.
  - Missing, duplicate, malformed, and ambiguous parquet.
  - Complete chunks continuing around incomplete chunks.
  - Verify-only proving the writer is never called.
  - Sequential call order and bounded payload sizes.
  - No-change, successful writes, read-back, and JSON-safe summaries.
  - Capacity forecast-version forwarding and stale-conflict propagation.
  - Source manifest/object mutation detection.
  - Independent post-write audit failure.
  - Same DAG arguments resolving to UAT on dev/QA and production on prod.
- Use injected readers/writers and mocked S3 throughout; make no live TSDB or OCCTO calls.
- Run focused tests first, then full Japan pytest, Ruff, docs checks, lock validation, Airflow migration/import checks, and repository scope/security checks.

---
name: delegate
description: Delegate work to a separate, sidebar-visible Codex task, keep it in the origin task's exact project when the origin belongs to one, return immediately, and have the delegated task message its result back to the origin. # Delegate

Create a normal peer task in the origin's exact project, if it has one. Let the origin finish its
dispatch turn immediately; have the peer start a new origin turn when its result is ready.

## Rules

- Treat `$delegate` or an explicit request for a new/separate Codex task as
  authorization to create it.
- Use Codex task tools, loading them with tool search when necessary.
- Require both `create_thread` and `send_message_to_thread` to be callable. If either is
  unavailable, explain that event-based delegation is unavailable and do not imitate it with
  polling.
- Use `create_thread`; never use `fork_thread`, subagent tools, worktrees, or cloud tasks.
- Treat target placement as a fail-closed invariant:
  - If the origin belongs to a saved project, create the delegated task in that exact project with
    `target: {type: "project", projectId: "…", environment: {type: "local"}}`.
  - If the origin is projectless, create a projectless task with
    `target: {type: "projectless"}`.
  - Never substitute a different project, infer a project from a filesystem path, or fall back
    between saved-project and projectless targets.
  - If the origin's project membership or exact `projectId` cannot be established, do not create
    the task. Explain the placement blocker.
- Preserve the user's scope, permissions, constraints, and completion criteria.
- Do not set a model or reasoning effort unless the user explicitly requests it.
- Do not archive the delegated task.

## Delegate

1. Resolve the origin's exact `threadId` and `hostId` from app context or `list_threads`. Read the
   origin task's own metadata to determine whether it belongs to a saved project; do not use the
   current directory, repository, recency, or a similarly named task as evidence of membership.
   For a saved-project origin, use `list_projects` as needed to resolve and cross-check its exact
   `projectId`. Stop if the identity or membership is ambiguous.
2. Generate a short unique delegation ID.
3. Write a self-contained worker prompt containing:
   - The requested work, relevant context and paths, constraints, and expected evidence.
   - The delegation ID plus the origin's exact `threadId` and `hostId`.
   - An instruction to call `send_message_to_thread` on the origin when the work reaches a
     terminal state. Send a concise, self-contained message containing the delegation ID,
     `completed`, `blocked`, or `failed`, the result, artifact paths or links, and the recommended
     next action.
   - An instruction to send `needs_input` with the exact question as soon as new user input or
     authority is required.
   - An instruction to keep ordinary progress in the worker task and send exactly one terminal
     result message to the origin.
4. Call `create_thread` once:
   - Saved project:
     `target: {type: "project", projectId: "…", environment: {type: "local"}}`
   - Projectless origin: `target: {type: "projectless"}`
   Pass the target explicitly; never rely on a default or inherited placement.
5. Retain the returned `threadId` and `hostId`. If the response exposes placement metadata, verify
   it matches the required target. Treat any mismatch as a failed delegation and report it; do not
   retry in another project or as projectless.
6. End the origin's dispatch turn immediately. Tell the user the task is running and will message
   this task when ready. Do not call `wait_threads` or repeatedly read the worker.
7. Emit `::created-thread{threadId="THREAD_ID"}` on its own line in the origin's final response.

## Handle Returned Messages

- Treat a message carrying the delegation ID as a result from the existing delegation, not as a
  request to create another task.
- On `needs_input`, surface the exact question. After the user answers, send the answer to the
  worker with `send_message_to_thread`; then finish the origin turn again.
- On `completed`, integrate the returned result and continue the parent objective if work remains.
- Report failures or incomplete work plainly.

</skill>

### User - 2026-07-30T05:15:13.902Z

thanks. This got me a wild idea, can we test (or should we even) both the smp-japan refactoring AND backfill at the same time? Bad idea?

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T05:15:31.279Z

Testing them in the same UAT deployment is sensible—actually unavoidable, because the backfill branch is stacked on the refactor. What would be risky is testing both with a full FY2025 write immediately.

Use one rebuilt image and deploy the stacked backfill branch, but validate in layers:

1. Confirm Airflow imports and `smp-common==0.6.0`.
2. Exercise the refactored recurring paths in verify-only mode:
   - one completed actual-flow day;
   - one bounded capacity snapshot.
3. Run the backfill DAG with a tiny historical range and `write=false`.
4. Write that tiny range—ideally one actual-flow day and one capacity day—then confirm exact read-back.
5. Rerun it and require `no-change`.
6. Run the full FY2025 backfill with `write=false`.
7. Review counts, gaps, source identities, and stale-version findings.
8. Only then run FY2025 with `write=true`.
9. Finish with another verify-only/no-change run.

This provides strong combined testing while retaining fault attribution:

- Failure in steps 2–5 points toward the refactor/common-runtime integration.
- Failure only at full-year preflight points toward source coverage or backfill validation.
- Failure only at scale points toward SDK limits, task duration, memory, or TSDB throughput.

One caution: don’t use the year-long backfill as the first live write through the refactored runtime. A one-day canary proves configuration, credentials, catalogue access, forecast-version behavior, writes, and read-back before exposing roughly 1.23 million points.

So: **same branch, image, and UAT window—yes. Same first operation—no.** This is probably the most efficient way to test both changes tomorrow.

# Daily Dump

Treat chat as the capture surface and `/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/inbox/YYYY-MM-DD.md` as the live readable note.

## Capture Workflow

1. Read `/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/AGENTS.md` and `/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/inbox/AGENTS.md`.
2. Resolve the capture date and time in `Asia/Manila`. Use today unless the user clearly identifies another date.
3. Open today's note. If it does not exist, create it from `assets/daily-note-template.md`, replacing the placeholders.
4. Interpret the new dump in the context of the existing note:
   - Add concrete commitments or follow-ups to `Today` as unchecked tasks.
   - When later evidence clearly completes a task, change it to `[x]`, strike through its text, and append a timestamped completion annotation.
   - Preserve cancelled or superseded tasks as completed, struck-through entries with the reason.
   - Mark blocked or waiting work without completing it.
   - Add observations, ideas, decisions, feelings, and non-actionable context to `Notes`.
   - Update an existing entry instead of creating a semantic duplicate.
5. Append the user's raw dump once under `Timeline`, using a timestamped subheading and Markdown blockquote. Preserve wording; do not silently correct names or claims.
6. Update `Last Updated` without changing the capture date.
7. Use `apply_patch` for edits. Do not run a full ingest, update wiki pages, edit `actions.md`, commit, or push unless the user explicitly asks.
8. Reply briefly with what changed and a clickable link to the daily note.

## State Rules

- Treat inferred daily tasks as provisional until ingest promotes unresolved work to `actions.md`.
- Infer routine state transitions when the evidence is clear. Ask only when marking the wrong task would materially distort the record.
- Use `UNCERTAIN:` for ambiguous owners, names, acronyms, dates, or high-impact interpretations.
- Never remove raw timeline entries.
- Never erase an earlier task merely because its state changed.
- Keep annotations short and chronological.
- If a dump contains both an update and a thought, update `Today`, add the thought to `Notes`, and preserve the full raw message in `Timeline`.

## Invocation Boundary

Explicit `$dump` invocation always activates this workflow. Implicit activation requires a clear capture signal such as `dump:` or an unambiguous request to log or remember something. A bare statement in an unrelated task is not enough evidence that the user wants it written to the Mind Palace.

</skill>

# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Baraluga Mind Agent Instructions

You are maintaining a local markdown second brain.

Your job is to turn raw captures into durable, useful wiki pages without inventing facts.

## Core Rules

- Read any nested `AGENTS.md` files that apply to the files being processed or edited.
- Preserve source material during ingest.
- Do not delete raw inputs unless explicitly asked.
- After processing an `inbox/` file, move it to the appropriate `sources/` folder when it remains useful as evidence.
- Leave an `inbox/` file in place when it still needs follow-up, review, or clarification.
- Do not fabricate details, links, names, dates, or decisions.
- Distinguish facts from inferences.
- Prefer small updates to existing wiki pages over creating duplicate notes.
- Link related wiki pages using Obsidian-style links like `[[concept-name]]`.
- Keep prose direct, dense, and useful.
- Use `UNCERTAIN:` when a claim needs verification.
- If source material is ambiguous, say so in the page.

## Actions

`actions.md` is the canonical register for follow-up work.

- Put every captured follow-up, task, owner commitment, or call to action in `actions.md`.
- Keep `actions.md` organized into exactly these top-level sections: `Today`, `Open`, `Waiting`, and `Done`.
- Add newly captured actions to `Open` by default.
- Use `Today` only when the user explicitly selects or prioritizes work for today.
- Use `Waiting` for actions blocked on another person, external answer, access, or event.
- Do not bury active TODOs inside wiki pages.
- Wiki pages may preserve context for an action, but the actionable checkbox belongs in `actions.md`.
- When adding an action, include a `Context` link to the related wiki page and a `Source` path to the captured evidence.
- Do not duplicate an existing open action; update it only when the new source adds material context.
- Move completed actions to `Done` instead of deleting them.
- Reconcile existing actions against new evidence during ingest. Move completed, superseded, or no-longer-relevant actions to `Done` with a brief source-backed reason; do not infer closure from ambiguous evidence.
- Source summaries may include non-canonical `Next Steps` when reflecting meeting content, but active tracking still belongs in `actions.md`.

## File Naming

Use lowercase kebab-case for all new files and directories.

Good:

- `wiki/concepts/second-brain.md`
- `wiki/projects/baraluga-mind.md`
- `wiki/decisions/2026-07-04-use-markdown-wiki.md`
- `inbox/2026-07-04-team-sync.md`

Avoid:

- `Second Brain.md`
- `second_brain.md`
- `Team Sync.md`
- `2026_07_04_notes.md`

## Process Improvement

Do not edit `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, or nested instruction files during normal ingest unless explicitly asked.

If you notice repeated friction, ambiguity, bad output, a useful convention, or instruction bloat, append a short note to `process-notes.md` instead.

Use this format:

```markdown
### YYYY-MM-DD

- Observation: ...
- Suggested rule: ...
- Example: ...
```

Instruction files should stay small and scoped. Prefer one local, source-specific instruction over expanding the root file when a rule applies only to one folder or content type.

## Ingest Behavior

When processing files from `inbox/` or `sources/`:

1. Identify the source type: article, meeting, voice note, PDF, pasted note, or mixed capture.
2. Extract durable entities:
   - people
   - projects
   - concepts
   - decisions
   - open questions
   - action items
3. Update existing pages when possible.
4. Create new pages only when the topic is likely to matter again.
5. Add or update captured action items in `actions.md`.
6. Add a short `Sources` section with the source filename or path.
7. Move processed `inbox/` files to the appropriate `sources/` subfolder when they remain useful as evidence:
   - `sources/articles/` for articles, links, and reading notes
   - `sources/meetings/` for meeting transcripts, agendas, and summaries
   - `sources/voice/` for voice-note transcripts and dictated thoughts
   - `sources/pdfs/` for PDFs and extracted PDF notes
8. Add a short `Last Updated` date using `YYYY-MM-DD`.
9. Validate the complete ingest diff, then commit and push all ingest changes using an unscoped conventional commit.

## Wiki Page Shape

Use this structure when creating a new durable page:

```markdown
# Page Title

## Summary

One to three paragraphs explaining the durable point of the page.

## Details

Concrete facts, context, links, tradeoffs, and examples.

## Open Questions

- UNCERTAIN: ...

## Sources

- `sources/...`

Last Updated: YYYY-MM-DD
```

If a section is not useful, omit it.

## Decision Page Shape

Use `wiki/decisions/YYYY-MM-DD-short-decision.md`.

```markdown
# Decision: Short Decision

## Decision

State the decision plainly.

## Context

Why this came up.

## Rationale

Why this option was chosen.

## Tradeoffs

What this makes easier or harder.

## Sources

- `sources/...`

Last Updated: YYYY-MM-DD
```

## Review Output

- pages created
- pages updated
- actions added or updated in `actions.md`
- source files processed
- uncertain claims
- new or suspicious names, acronyms, systems, projects, or jargon needing confirmation
- suggested next cleanup

Also include a consolidated handover section titled `Needs Confirmation`.

`Needs Confirmation` should be pleasant to scan and useful as a one-by-one clarification queue for the user. It should include only:

- all current needs-confirmation items from the ingest review, including suspicious names, acronyms, systems, projects, jargon, ambiguous owners, and uncertain high-impact claims.

Do not include the full `Open` action register in this handover by default. `actions.md` remains the canonical action register, but many captured TODOs may belong to other people or teams and should not be presented as Brian's assignment unless the user explicitly asks.

Format `Needs Confirmation` as grouped checklists by durable area when possible, such as `Team Operations`, `SMP Platform`, `Japan Interconnector`, and `Alerting and Ops`.

For each item, include:

- the exact term or claim to confirm;
- why confirmation is needed;
- the source path or related wiki page.

Keep `Needs Confirmation` concise enough to work through manually. Exclude items that are merely low-priority, already resolved, or not currently worth asking the user about.

# Consult Mind Palace

Treat `/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind` as the canonical root. Use absolute paths in commands so the workflow works from any current directory.

## Search Workflow

1. Confirm the root exists. If it does not, report the missing path and stop.
2. Read `/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/AGENTS.md` and any nested `AGENTS.md` that applies to files being read or changed.
3. Translate the request into search terms: the exact phrase, obvious spelling or acronym variants, related project and person names, and likely Obsidian page slugs. Do not invent speculative synonyms.
4. Search filenames, headings, body text, and Obsidian links with `rg`. Start with `wiki/` and `actions.md`, then search `sources/` for evidence and details. Search `inbox/` only when the user asks for exhaustive or very recent knowledge, and label unprocessed material clearly.
5. Open the most relevant durable pages in full. Follow their `[[wiki-links]]` and `Sources` references when they materially expand or verify the answer.
6. Check `actions.md` for topic-related work. Treat it as the canonical action register; do not infer that every action belongs to the user.
7. Inspect relevant source passages directly before making detailed claims. For transcript-derived material, preserve uncertainty around unfamiliar names, acronyms, owners, dates, and jargon.
8. Stop when additional matches are duplicates or add no material context. If the query is ambiguous, present the likely interpretation and mention the uncovered alternative instead of silently choosing.

Example discovery commands:

```bash
rg --files /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/wiki /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/sources /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/inbox
rg -n -i --glob '*.md' 'term|variant' /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/wiki /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/actions.md
rg -n -i --glob '*.md' 'term|variant' /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/sources
```

Use quoted patterns and `rg -F` when search terms contain regular-expression characters.

## Evidence Model

Apply this precedence without hiding disagreement:

- `wiki/`: durable synthesized knowledge; use as the organizing layer.
- `wiki/decisions/`: explicit decisions; distinguish them from proposals or discussion.
- `actions.md`: canonical status of follow-up work.
- `sources/`: captured evidence and finer detail; use to verify and enrich durable pages.
- `inbox/`: unprocessed captures; treat as provisional.

State facts as facts only when supported. Label interpretations as inferences. Preserve `UNCERTAIN:` claims and surface conflicting or stale-looking evidence with dates and paths. Never fabricate missing context.

## Response Shape

Adapt the depth to the request. For broad requests such as “everything we know about X,” provide:

1. A concise synthesis suitable for the user's immediate purpose.
2. Relevant facts and context, grouped by durable theme rather than by file.
3. Explicit decisions and rationale.
4. Open actions, open questions, conflicts, and uncertain claims, each clearly labeled.
5. A short source trail containing the most useful wiki pages and primary captures.

Prefer direct prose over a raw search-results dump. Include relevant dates. Cite repository-relative paths in backticks and, when useful in Codex, make key local files clickable using their absolute paths. Say plainly when the mind palace contains little or no relevant information.

For writing support, first return a source-backed briefing tailored to the requested artifact. Draft the artifact only when the user asks for a draft or when drafting is clearly part of the request.

## Mutation Boundary

Default to read-only consultation. Do not edit, ingest, reorganize, commit, or push the mind palace unless the user explicitly asks. If mutation is requested, follow all applicable repository instructions, preserve raw inputs, and validate the complete diff before reporting completion.

</skill>

# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/repos/smp

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# AGENTS.md

This file provides guidance to AI coding agents working under `/Users/qn5792/repos/smp`.

## Overview

`/smp` is a parent directory, not a monorepo. It contains separate repositories for the **SMP (Scraper Management Platform)** and adjacent APAC scraper work. Read the repo-local `AGENTS.md` before changing files inside any child repo.

## Current Repos

| Repo | Purpose | Tooling |
|---|---|---|
| `scraper-scripts` | Raw standalone scraper scripts and behavioral references before Airflow adaptation | Python 3.13, `uv` |
| `smp-india` | India-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-japan` | Japan-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-tool` | Airflow Docker image, Helm values, Kubernetes scripts, Okta auth, Minikube/EKS setup | Python 3.12, `uv`, Helm, Kubernetes |
| `smp-dashboard` | Grafana dashboards and `cdh-dataset-manager` for CommonDataHub datasets | Python 3.11, `uv` |
| `apac-tsdb-scraper` | Poetry/solaris-sfw TSDB scrapers deployed through the older Lambda/tag flow | Python 3.12, Poetry |

Legacy names still appear in older docs and code comments:

- `scraper-dags` is now split into `smp-india` and `smp-japan`.
- `scraper-tool` is now `smp-tool`.
- `scraper-dashboard` is now `smp-dashboard`.

## End-to-End SMP Flow

```text
scraper-scripts  ->  smp-india / smp-japan  ->  smp-tool  ->  smp-dashboard
(reference impl)     (Airflow DAGs)             (runtime)     (CDH + Grafana)
```

Use `scraper-scripts` as the behavioral source when porting or validating a DAG. Region-specific DAG implementation now belongs in either `smp-india` or `smp-japan`; shared Airflow/platform behavior should come from `smp-common` or `smp-tool`, not be copied between region repos.

## Shared Rules

- Do not commit credentials, tokens, kubeconfigs, generated local secrets, or one-off path rewrites.
- Private dependencies come from ENGIE Artifactory. Prefer fixing local credentials over changing package indexes.
- Keep region boundaries explicit: India DAG code in `smp-india`, Japan DAG code in `smp-japan`.
- For Airflow DAGs, keep heavy imports out of module scope because Airflow parses DAG files frequently.
- For dashboard work, verify the upstream parquet/S3/CDH shape before changing Grafana JSON or dataset definitions.
- For infra changes, call out whether the change affects image build, Helm values, auth, DAG sync, runtime secrets, or Kubernetes namespaces.

## Cross-Repo Workflow: Porting A Script To A DAG

1. Run and observe the source script in `scraper-scripts`; capture inputs, outputs, credentials, file names, date logic, and failure behavior.
2. Choose the DAG repo by market ownership: `smp-india` or `smp-japan`.
3. Check whether an existing DAG for the same source should be extended before creating a new DAG.
4. Implement under `dags/src/`, keeping task runtime scraper modules under `dags/src/scripts/`.
5. Validate locally with `ruff`, `pytest`, and, when practical, `airflow dags test`.
6. Coordinate deployment through the appropriate branch/environment and `smp-tool` git-sync/Helm configuration.
7. Update `smp-dashboard` only after the DAG output is stable and visible where CDH/Grafana expects it.

## Branch And Environment Notes

- GitHub repos commonly use `main`; some DAG deployment flows still reference `dev`, `qa`, `master`, or region-specific branches.
- `smp-tool` has region-aware config under `config/japan/<env>/` and `config/india/<env>/`.
- Airflow namespaces are region-specific in current scripts, for example `smp-dev`, `smp-qa`, `smp-prod`, `smp-india-dev`, `smp-india-qa`, and `smp-india-prod`.
- Treat any old `scraper-dags`/ADO git-sync reference as potentially stale and verify against current `smp-tool` config before editing deployment behavior.

</INSTRUCTIONS>

<proposed_plan>
# SCR-1216 — Disable Selected Japan HJKS Report Emails

## Summary

Permanently remove the three report-email flows visible in Hiromi’s screenshot while preserving scraping, chart generation, parquet output, CDH uploads, orchestration, and Teams failure alerts.

Estimated implementation effort: 2–4 hours, or roughly half a developer-day including focused validation.

## Implementation Changes

- Remove the email tasks for:
  - `JAPAN - 2Y charts - Energy Availability and Demand Analysis`
  - `JAPAN - Nuclear Generation by Region` — both short-horizon and multi-year emails
  - `JAPAN - Energy Availability Analysis Residual Demand` — all four date-based emails
- Rewire each DAG so processing continues directly to its existing upload and completion tasks.
- For the date-based HJKS DAG, preserve the direct processing-to-end dependency so a failed scrape cannot be masked by the upload task’s `ALL_DONE` trigger rule.
- Remove email-only helper functions, constants, imports, and outdated DAG descriptions made unreachable by this change.
- Do not change the global `email_enabled` variable or `JAPAN_EMAIL_RECIPIENTS`; unrelated Japan report emails must continue normally.

## Interfaces and Configuration

- No new Airflow variables, runtime switches, APIs, schemas, or dataset changes.
- Existing DAG IDs, schedules, orchestrator triggers, output paths, and alert callbacks remain unchanged.

## Test and Rollout Plan

- Add DAG-topology assertions confirming the three DAGs contain no report-email tasks and retain their processing, upload, and completion paths.
- Confirm the date-based DAG still records a failed scrape as a failed DAG run while allowing its cleanup/upload behavior.
- Run focused tests for the three affected DAGs, then the repository Ruff and DAG test suites.
- Promote through the normal `dev → qa → prod` process.
- After production deployment, observe one scheduled orchestrator cycle and confirm:
  - all three datasets still process and upload successfully;
  - none of the three email subject families is emitted;
  - unrelated HJKS emails and Teams failure alerts remain available.

## Assumptions

- SCR-1216 covers exactly the three report types shown in the screenshot.
- Deactivation is permanent in code and reversible through version control, rather than controlled by an operational switch.
- Historical emails are unaffected; the change only prevents future sends after deployment.
</proposed_plan>

<skill>
<name>delegate</name>
<path>/Users/qn5792/.codex/skills/delegate/SKILL.md</path>
---
name: delegate
description: Delegate work to a separate, sidebar-visible Codex task, keep it in the origin task's exact project when the origin belongs to one, return immediately, and have the delegated task message its result back to the origin. Use only when the user explicitly asks for a new, main, peer, or separate Codex task that reports back, or explicitly invokes $delegate. Do not use for ordinary subagent requests, unsolicited delegation, monitoring an existing task, or forking.
---

# Delegate

Create a normal peer task in the origin's exact project, if it has one. Let the origin finish its
dispatch turn immediately; have the peer start a new origin turn when its result is ready.

## Rules

- Treat `$delegate` or an explicit request for a new/separate Codex task as
  authorization to create it.
- Use Codex task tools, loading them with tool search when necessary.
- Require both `create_thread` and `send_message_to_thread` to be callable. If either is
  unavailable, explain that event-based delegation is unavailable and do not imitate it with
  polling.
- Use `create_thread`; never use `fork_thread`, subagent tools, worktrees, or cloud tasks.
- Treat target placement as a fail-closed invariant:
  - If the origin belongs to a saved project, create the delegated task in that exact project with
    `target: {type: "project", projectId: "…", environment: {type: "local"}}`.
  - If the origin is projectless, create a projectless task with
    `target: {type: "projectless"}`.
  - Never substitute a different project, infer a project from a filesystem path, or fall back
    between saved-project and projectless targets.
  - If the origin's project membership or exact `projectId` cannot be established, do not create
    the task. Explain the placement blocker.
- Preserve the user's scope, permissions, constraints, and completion criteria.
- Do not set a model or reasoning effort unless the user explicitly requests it.
- Do not archive the delegated task.

## Delegate

1. Resolve the origin's exact `threadId` and `hostId` from app context or `list_threads`. Read the
   origin task's own metadata to determine whether it belongs to a saved project; do not use the
   current directory, repository, recency, or a similarly named task as evidence of membership.
   For a saved-project origin, use `list_projects` as needed to resolve and cross-check its exact
   `projectId`. Stop if the identity or membership is ambiguous.
2. Generate a short unique delegation ID.
3. Write a self-contained worker prompt containing:
   - The requested work, relevant context and paths, constraints, and expected evidence.
   - The delegation ID plus the origin's exact `threadId` and `hostId`.
   - An instruction to call `send_message_to_thread` on the origin when the work reaches a
     terminal state. Send a concise, self-contained message containing the delegation ID,
     `completed`, `blocked`, or `failed`, the result, artifact paths or links, and the recommended
     next action.
   - An instruction to send `needs_input` with the exact question as soon as new user input or
     authority is required.
   - An instruction to keep ordinary progress in the worker task and send exactly one terminal
     result message to the origin.
4. Call `create_thread` once:
   - Saved project:
     `target: {type: "project", projectId: "…", environment: {type: "local"}}`
   - Projectless origin: `target: {type: "projectless"}`
   Pass the target explicitly; never rely on a default or inherited placement.
5. Retain the returned `threadId` and `hostId`. If the response exposes placement metadata, verify
   it matches the required target. Treat any mismatch as a failed delegation and report it; do not
   retry in another project or as projectless.
6. End the origin's dispatch turn immediately. Tell the user the task is running and will message
   this task when ready. Do not call `wait_threads` or repeatedly read the worker.
7. Emit `::created-thread{threadId="THREAD_ID"}` on its own line in the origin's final response.

## Handle Returned Messages

- Treat a message carrying the delegation ID as a result from the existing delegation, not as a
  request to create another task.
- On `needs_input`, surface the exact question. After the user answers, send the answer to the
  worker with `send_message_to_thread`; then finish the origin turn again.
- On `completed`, integrate the returned result and continue the parent objective if work remains.
- Report failures or incomplete work plainly.

</skill>

   - `send_multi_year_email`
   - `send_emails_task`

In prod, promote `qa → prod` before a monitored scheduled cycle. Confirm the new revision is synced and no old runs are still executing, then observe one complete cycle:

- The three DAGs succeed.
- CDH/output uploads still complete.
- No new emails with the three subject families appear.
- Unrelated Japan emails and Teams failure alerts still work normally.

- Deployed DAG graph contains no email tasks.
- Successful downstream uploads.
- Mailbox confirms no matching messages for a post-deployment run.

One caveat: a DAG run already active before git-sync could still use the previous task definition and send an email. Only runs created after the new revision is loaded should count toward acceptance.

# AGENTS.md

This file provides guidance to AI coding agents working under `/Users/qn5792/repos/smp`.

`/smp` is a parent directory, not a monorepo. It contains separate repositories for the **SMP (Scraper Management Platform)** and adjacent APAC scraper work. Read the repo-local `AGENTS.md` before changing files inside any child repo.

| Repo | Purpose | Tooling |
|---|---|---|
| `scraper-scripts` | Raw standalone scraper scripts and behavioral references before Airflow adaptation | Python 3.13, `uv` |
| `smp-india` | India-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-japan` | Japan-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-tool` | Airflow Docker image, Helm values, Kubernetes scripts, Okta auth, Minikube/EKS setup | Python 3.12, `uv`, Helm, Kubernetes |
| `smp-dashboard` | Grafana dashboards and `cdh-dataset-manager` for CommonDataHub datasets | Python 3.11, `uv` |
| `apac-tsdb-scraper` | Poetry/solaris-sfw TSDB scrapers deployed through the older Lambda/tag flow | Python 3.12, Poetry |

Legacy names still appear in older docs and code comments:

- `scraper-dags` is now split into `smp-india` and `smp-japan`.
- `scraper-tool` is now `smp-tool`.
- `scraper-dashboard` is now `smp-dashboard`.

## End-to-End SMP Flow

```text
scraper-scripts  ->  smp-india / smp-japan  ->  smp-tool  ->  smp-dashboard
(reference impl)     (Airflow DAGs)             (runtime)     (CDH + Grafana)
```

Use `scraper-scripts` as the behavioral source when porting or validating a DAG. Region-specific DAG implementation now belongs in either `smp-india` or `smp-japan`; shared Airflow/platform behavior should come from `smp-common` or `smp-tool`, not be copied between region repos.

## Shared Rules

- Do not commit credentials, tokens, kubeconfigs, generated local secrets, or one-off path rewrites.
- Private dependencies come from ENGIE Artifactory. Prefer fixing local credentials over changing package indexes.
- Keep region boundaries explicit: India DAG code in `smp-india`, Japan DAG code in `smp-japan`.
- For Airflow DAGs, keep heavy imports out of module scope because Airflow parses DAG files frequently.
- For dashboard work, verify the upstream parquet/S3/CDH shape before changing Grafana JSON or dataset definitions.
- For infra changes, call out whether the change affects image build, Helm values, auth, DAG sync, runtime secrets, or Kubernetes namespaces.

## Cross-Repo Workflow: Porting A Script To A DAG

1. Run and observe the source script in `scraper-scripts`; capture inputs, outputs, credentials, file names, date logic, and failure behavior.
2. Choose the DAG repo by market ownership: `smp-india` or `smp-japan`.
3. Check whether an existing DAG for the same source should be extended before creating a new DAG.
4. Implement under `dags/src/`, keeping task runtime scraper modules under `dags/src/scripts/`.
5. Validate locally with `ruff`, `pytest`, and, when practical, `airflow dags test`.
6. Coordinate deployment through the appropriate branch/environment and `smp-tool` git-sync/Helm configuration.
7. Update `smp-dashboard` only after the DAG output is stable and visible where CDH/Grafana expects it.

## Branch And Environment Notes

- GitHub repos commonly use `main`; some DAG deployment flows still reference `dev`, `qa`, `master`, or region-specific branches.
- `smp-tool` has region-aware config under `config/japan/<env>/` and `config/india/<env>/`.
- Airflow namespaces are region-specific in current scripts, for example `smp-dev`, `smp-qa`, `smp-prod`, `smp-india-dev`, `smp-india-qa`, and `smp-india-prod`.
- Treat any old `scraper-dags`/ADO git-sync reference as potentially stale and verify against current `smp-tool` config before editing deployment behavior.

# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Baraluga Mind Agent Instructions

You are maintaining a local markdown second brain.

Your job is to turn raw captures into durable, useful wiki pages without inventing facts.

## Core Rules

- Read any nested `AGENTS.md` files that apply to the files being processed or edited.
- Preserve source material during ingest.
- Do not delete raw inputs unless explicitly asked.
- After processing an `inbox/` file, move it to the appropriate `sources/` folder when it remains useful as evidence.
- Leave an `inbox/` file in place when it still needs follow-up, review, or clarification.
- Do not fabricate details, links, names, dates, or decisions.
- Distinguish facts from inferences.
- Prefer small updates to existing wiki pages over creating duplicate notes.
- Link related wiki pages using Obsidian-style links like `[[concept-name]]`.
- Keep prose direct, dense, and useful.
- Use `UNCERTAIN:` when a claim needs verification.
- If source material is ambiguous, say so in the page.

## Actions

`actions.md` is the canonical register for follow-up work.

- Put every captured follow-up, task, owner commitment, or call to action in `actions.md`.
- Keep `actions.md` organized into exactly these top-level sections: `Today`, `Open`, `Waiting`, and `Done`.
- Add newly captured actions to `Open` by default.
- Use `Today` only when the user explicitly selects or prioritizes work for today.
- Use `Waiting` for actions blocked on another person, external answer, access, or event.
- Do not bury active TODOs inside wiki pages.
- Wiki pages may preserve context for an action, but the actionable checkbox belongs in `actions.md`.
- When adding an action, include a `Context` link to the related wiki page and a `Source` path to the captured evidence.
- Do not duplicate an existing open action; update it only when the new source adds material context.
- Move completed actions to `Done` instead of deleting them.
- Reconcile existing actions against new evidence during ingest. Move completed, superseded, or no-longer-relevant actions to `Done` with a brief source-backed reason; do not infer closure from ambiguous evidence.
- Source summaries may include non-canonical `Next Steps` when reflecting meeting content, but active tracking still belongs in `actions.md`.

## File Naming

Use lowercase kebab-case for all new files and directories.

Good:

- `wiki/concepts/second-brain.md`
- `wiki/projects/baraluga-mind.md`
- `wiki/decisions/2026-07-04-use-markdown-wiki.md`
- `inbox/2026-07-04-team-sync.md`

Avoid:

- `Second Brain.md`
- `second_brain.md`
- `Team Sync.md`
- `2026_07_04_notes.md`

## Process Improvement

Do not edit `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, or nested instruction files during normal ingest unless explicitly asked.

If you notice repeated friction, ambiguity, bad output, a useful convention, or instruction bloat, append a short note to `process-notes.md` instead.

Use this format:

```markdown
### YYYY-MM-DD

- Observation: ...
- Suggested rule: ...
- Example: ...
```

Instruction files should stay small and scoped. Prefer one local, source-specific instruction over expanding the root file when a rule applies only to one folder or content type.

## Ingest Behavior

When processing files from `inbox/` or `sources/`:

1. Identify the source type: article, meeting, voice note, PDF, pasted note, or mixed capture.
2. Extract durable entities:
   - people
   - projects
   - concepts
   - decisions
   - open questions
   - action items
3. Update existing pages when possible.
4. Create new pages only when the topic is likely to matter again.
5. Add or update captured action items in `actions.md`.
6. Add a short `Sources` section with the source filename or path.
7. Move processed `inbox/` files to the appropriate `sources/` subfolder when they remain useful as evidence:
   - `sources/articles/` for articles, links, and reading notes
   - `sources/meetings/` for meeting transcripts, agendas, and summaries
   - `sources/voice/` for voice-note transcripts and dictated thoughts
   - `sources/pdfs/` for PDFs and extracted PDF notes
8. Add a short `Last Updated` date using `YYYY-MM-DD`.
9. Validate the complete ingest diff, then commit and push all ingest changes using an unscoped conventional commit.

## Wiki Page Shape

Use this structure when creating a new durable page:

```markdown
# Page Title

## Summary

One to three paragraphs explaining the durable point of the page.

## Details

Concrete facts, context, links, tradeoffs, and examples.

## Open Questions

- UNCERTAIN: ...

## Sources

- `sources/...`

Last Updated: YYYY-MM-DD
```

If a section is not useful, omit it.

## Decision Page Shape

Use `wiki/decisions/YYYY-MM-DD-short-decision.md`.

```markdown
# Decision: Short Decision

## Decision

State the decision plainly.

## Context

Why this came up.

## Rationale

Why this option was chosen.

## Tradeoffs

What this makes easier or harder.

## Sources

- `sources/...`

Last Updated: YYYY-MM-DD
```

## Review Output

After an ingest pass, report:

- pages created
- pages updated
- actions added or updated in `actions.md`
- source files processed
- uncertain claims
- new or suspicious names, acronyms, systems, projects, or jargon needing confirmation
- suggested next cleanup

Also include a consolidated handover section titled `Needs Confirmation`.

`Needs Confirmation` should be pleasant to scan and useful as a one-by-one clarification queue for the user. It should include only:

- all current needs-confirmation items from the ingest review, including suspicious names, acronyms, systems, projects, jargon, ambiguous owners, and uncertain high-impact claims.

Do not include the full `Open` action register in this handover by default. `actions.md` remains the canonical action register, but many captured TODOs may belong to other people or teams and should not be presented as Brian's assignment unless the user explicitly asks.

Format `Needs Confirmation` as grouped checklists by durable area when possible, such as `Team Operations`, `SMP Platform`, `Japan Interconnector`, and `Alerting and Ops`.

For each item, include:

- the exact term or claim to confirm;
- why confirmation is needed;
- the source path or related wiki page.

Keep `Needs Confirmation` concise enough to work through manually. Exclude items that are merely low-priority, already resolved, or not currently worth asking the user about.

### User - 2026-07-30T04:08:33.133Z

Ah. How do we avoid this next time then?

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T04:08:48.134Z

The exporter needs a size guard; otherwise the next daily capture may recreate the problem.

Best approach:

- Keep the daily `.md` as a lightweight index and summary.
- Store raw conversation text as `.txt`, which Obsidian won’t parse.
- Split raw exports by thread or session instead of producing one multi-megabyte document.
- Refuse to write any Markdown file larger than about 750 KB–1 MB.
- Deduplicate repeated snapshots of the same Codex thread.

The important part is enforcing this inside `scripts/export-codex-conversations.py`, not relying on manual cleanup afterward. I’d recommend a hard limit that automatically converts an oversized export into:

```text
2026-07-24-codex-conversations.md       # small index
2026-07-24-codex-conversations/         # raw transcript parts
  thread-001.txt
  thread-002.txt
  ...
```

That keeps the evidence intact, makes the index usable in Obsidian, and prevents this crash from recurring.

# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Baraluga Mind Agent Instructions

You are maintaining a local markdown second brain.

Your job is to turn raw captures into durable, useful wiki pages without inventing facts.

## Core Rules

- Read any nested `AGENTS.md` files that apply to the files being processed or edited.
- Preserve source material during ingest.
- Do not delete raw inputs unless explicitly asked.
- After processing an `inbox/` file, move it to the appropriate `sources/` folder when it remains useful as evidence.
- Leave an `inbox/` file in place when it still needs follow-up, review, or clarification.
- Do not fabricate details, links, names, dates, or decisions.
- Distinguish facts from inferences.
- Prefer small updates to existing wiki pages over creating duplicate notes.
- Link related wiki pages using Obsidian-style links like `[[concept-name]]`.
- Keep prose direct, dense, and useful.
- Use `UNCERTAIN:` when a claim needs verification.
- If source material is ambiguous, say so in the page.

## Actions

`actions.md` is the canonical register for follow-up work.

- Put every captured follow-up, task, owner commitment, or call to action in `actions.md`.
- Keep `actions.md` organized into exactly these top-level sections: `Today`, `Open`, `Waiting`, and `Done`.
- Add newly captured actions to `Open` by default.
- Use `Today` only when the user explicitly selects or prioritizes work for today.
- Use `Waiting` for actions blocked on another person, external answer, access, or event.
- Do not bury active TODOs inside wiki pages.
- Wiki pages may preserve context for an action, but the actionable checkbox belongs in `actions.md`.
- When adding an action, include a `Context` link to the related wiki page and a `Source` path to the captured evidence.
- Do not duplicate an existing open action; update it only when the new source adds material context.
- Move completed actions to `Done` instead of deleting them.
- Reconcile existing actions against new evidence during ingest. Move completed, superseded, or no-longer-relevant actions to `Done` with a brief source-backed reason; do not infer closure from ambiguous evidence.
- Source summaries may include non-canonical `Next Steps` when reflecting meeting content, but active tracking still belongs in `actions.md`.

## File Naming

Use lowercase kebab-case for all new files and directories.

Good:

- `wiki/concepts/second-brain.md`
- `wiki/projects/baraluga-mind.md`
- `wiki/decisions/2026-07-04-use-markdown-wiki.md`
- `inbox/2026-07-04-team-sync.md`

Avoid:

- `Second Brain.md`
- `second_brain.md`
- `Team Sync.md`
- `2026_07_04_notes.md`

## Process Improvement

Do not edit `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, or nested instruction files during normal ingest unless explicitly asked.

If you notice repeated friction, ambiguity, bad output, a useful convention, or instruction bloat, append a short note to `process-notes.md` instead.

Use this format:

```markdown
### YYYY-MM-DD

- Observation: ...
- Suggested rule: ...
- Example: ...
```

Instruction files should stay small and scoped. Prefer one local, source-specific instruction over expanding the root file when a rule applies only to one folder or content type.

## Ingest Behavior

When processing files from `inbox/` or `sources/`:

1. Identify the source type: article, meeting, voice note, PDF, pasted note, or mixed capture.
2. Extract durable entities:
   - people
   - projects
   - concepts
   - decisions
   - open questions
   - action items
3. Update existing pages when possible.
4. Create new pages only when the topic is likely to matter again.
5. Add or update captured action items in `actions.md`.
6. Add a short `Sources` section with the source filename or path.
7. Move processed `inbox/` files to the appropriate `sources/` subfolder when they remain useful as evidence:
   - `sources/articles/` for articles, links, and reading notes
   - `sources/meetings/` for meeting transcripts, agendas, and summaries
   - `sources/voice/` for voice-note transcripts and dictated thoughts
   - `sources/pdfs/` for PDFs and extracted PDF notes
8. Add a short `Last Updated` date using `YYYY-MM-DD`.
9. Validate the complete ingest diff, then commit and push all ingest changes using an unscoped conventional commit.

## Wiki Page Shape

Use this structure when creating a new durable page:

```markdown
# Page Title

## Summary

One to three paragraphs explaining the durable point of the page.

## Details

Concrete facts, context, links, tradeoffs, and examples.

## Open Questions

- UNCERTAIN: ...

## Sources

- `sources/...`

Last Updated: YYYY-MM-DD
```

If a section is not useful, omit it.

## Decision Page Shape

Use `wiki/decisions/YYYY-MM-DD-short-decision.md`.

```markdown
# Decision: Short Decision

## Decision

State the decision plainly.

## Context

Why this came up.

## Rationale

Why this option was chosen.

## Tradeoffs

What this makes easier or harder.

## Sources

- `sources/...`

Last Updated: YYYY-MM-DD
```

## Review Output

After an ingest pass, report:

- pages created
- pages updated
- actions added or updated in `actions.md`
- source files processed
- uncertain claims
- new or suspicious names, acronyms, systems, projects, or jargon needing confirmation
- suggested next cleanup

Also include a consolidated handover section titled `Needs Confirmation`.

`Needs Confirmation` should be pleasant to scan and useful as a one-by-one clarification queue for the user. It should include only:

- all current needs-confirmation items from the ingest review, including suspicious names, acronyms, systems, projects, jargon, ambiguous owners, and uncertain high-impact claims.

Do not include the full `Open` action register in this handover by default. `actions.md` remains the canonical action register, but many captured TODOs may belong to other people or teams and should not be presented as Brian's assignment unless the user explicitly asks.

Format `Needs Confirmation` as grouped checklists by durable area when possible, such as `Team Operations`, `SMP Platform`, `Japan Interconnector`, and `Alerting and Ops`.

For each item, include:

- the exact term or claim to confirm;
- why confirmation is needed;
- the source path or related wiki page.

Keep `Needs Confirmation` concise enough to work through manually. Exclude items that are merely low-priority, already resolved, or not currently worth asking the user about.

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T05:09:43.240Z

Yes. You want a **stateful daily note**, not merely a transcript of what you said.

I’d revise the workflow:

> Chat is the input. The daily note is the live interface. The nightly transcript is only the safety net.

On the first dump of the day, the pinned task creates `inbox/YYYY-MM-DD.md`. Every subsequent message immediately updates that same note.

For example:

```markdown
# 2026-07-30

## Today

- [x] ~~Follow up with Carlos about TSDB approval~~
  - 09:14 — Inferred as a TODO.
  - 15:42 — Done: Carlos approved the changes.

- [ ] Prepare the migration update
  - 11:08 — Inferred from discussion about tomorrow’s review.

## Notes

- 10:26 — Migration checklist should distinguish Git migration from pipeline migration.
- 14:10 — Certificate debugging took most of the afternoon.

## Timeline

- 09:14 — Need to follow up with Carlos today.
- 15:42 — Carlos approved it. Laurent can proceed.
```

The important behavior:

- New inferred action → add unchecked checkbox.
- Later inferred completion → change it to `[x]`, strike it out, and annotate when/why.
- Changed plan → preserve the old item but mark it superseded.
- Ordinary thought → add it under Notes.
- Every raw dump → preserve it in the Timeline.
- `actions.md` remains the long-term canonical register; the daily note is today’s readable projection of it.

Then, after ingestion, the file moves to `sources/notes/YYYY-MM-DD.md` as the finished journal for that date.

This is much closer to what you actually want: **you journal conversationally, but consume the result visually as an evolving annotated note**. And yes—the pinned task should update the note immediately rather than waiting for the nightly capture.

# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind

<INSTRUCTIONS>
These AGENTS.md instructions replace all previously provided AGENTS.md instructions.

# Baraluga Mind Agent Instructions

You are maintaining a local markdown second brain.

Your job is to turn raw captures into durable, useful wiki pages without inventing facts.

## Core Rules

- Read any nested `AGENTS.md` files that apply to the files being processed or edited.
- Preserve source material during ingest.
- Do not delete raw inputs unless explicitly asked.
- After processing an `inbox/` file, move it to the appropriate `sources/` folder when it remains useful as evidence.
- Leave an `inbox/` file in place when it still needs follow-up, review, or clarification.
- Do not fabricate details, links, names, dates, or decisions.
- Distinguish facts from inferences.
- Prefer small updates to existing wiki pages over creating duplicate notes.
- Link related wiki pages using Obsidian-style links like `[[concept-name]]`.
- Keep prose direct, dense, and useful.
- Use `UNCERTAIN:` when a claim needs verification.
- If source material is ambiguous, say so in the page.

## Actions

`actions.md` is the canonical register for follow-up work.

- Put every captured follow-up, task, owner commitment, or call to action in `actions.md`.
- Keep `actions.md` organized into exactly these top-level sections: `Today`, `Open`, `Waiting`, and `Done`.
- Add newly captured actions to `Open` by default.
- Use `Today` only when the user explicitly selects or prioritizes work for today.
- Use `Waiting` for actions blocked on another person, external answer, access, or event.
- Do not bury active TODOs inside wiki pages.
- Wiki pages may preserve context for an action, but the actionable checkbox belongs in `actions.md`.
- When adding an action, include a `Context` link to the related wiki page and a `Source` path to the captured evidence.
- Do not duplicate an existing open action; update it only when the new source adds material context.
- Move completed actions to `Done` instead of deleting them.
- Reconcile existing actions against new evidence during ingest. Move completed, superseded, or no-longer-relevant actions to `Done` with a brief source-backed reason; do not infer closure from ambiguous evidence.
- Source summaries may include non-canonical `Next Steps` when reflecting meeting content, but active tracking still belongs in `actions.md`.

## File Naming

Use lowercase kebab-case for all new files and directories.

Good:

- `wiki/concepts/second-brain.md`
- `wiki/projects/baraluga-mind.md`
- `wiki/decisions/2026-07-04-use-markdown-wiki.md`
- `inbox/2026-07-04-team-sync.md`

Avoid:

- `Second Brain.md`
- `second_brain.md`
- `Team Sync.md`
- `2026_07_04_notes.md`

## Process Improvement

Do not edit `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, or nested instruction files during normal ingest unless explicitly asked.

If you notice repeated friction, ambiguity, bad output, a useful convention, or instruction bloat, append a short note to `process-notes.md` instead.

Use this format:

```markdown
### YYYY-MM-DD

- Observation: ...
- Suggested rule: ...
- Example: ...
```

Instruction files should stay small and scoped. Prefer one local, source-specific instruction over expanding the root file when a rule applies only to one folder or content type.

## Ingest Behavior

When processing files from `inbox/` or `sources/`:

1. Identify the source type: article, meeting, voice note, PDF, pasted note, or mixed capture.
2. Extract durable entities:
   - people
   - projects
   - concepts
   - decisions
   - open questions
   - action items
3. Update existing pages when possible.
4. Create new pages only when the topic is likely to matter again.
5. Add or update captured action items in `actions.md`.
6. Add a short `Sources` section with the source filename or path.
7. Move processed `inbox/` files to the appropriate `sources/` subfolder when they remain useful as evidence:
   - `sources/articles/` for articles, links, and reading notes
   - `sources/meetings/` for meeting transcripts, agendas, and summaries
   - `sources/voice/` for voice-note transcripts and dictated thoughts
   - `sources/pdfs/` for PDFs and extracted PDF notes
   - `sources/notes/` for manual or AI-maintained daily notes
   - Leave the current Asia/Manila day's live daily note in `inbox/` unless the user explicitly asks to close or ingest it.
   - For a live `inbox/YYYY-MM-DD.md` daily note, reconcile its provisional task states with `actions.md`, then preserve it in `sources/notes/`.
   - When a daily note duplicates content in a Codex conversation export, prefer the structured daily note and use the transcript as backup evidence.
8. Add a short `Last Updated` date using `YYYY-MM-DD`.
9. Validate the complete ingest diff, then commit and push all ingest changes using an unscoped conventional commit.

## Wiki Page Shape

Use this structure when creating a new durable page:

```markdown
# Page Title

## Summary

One to three paragraphs explaining the durable point of the page.

## Details

Concrete facts, context, links, tradeoffs, and examples.

## Open Questions

- UNCERTAIN: ...

## Sources

- `sources/...`

Last Updated: YYYY-MM-DD
```

If a section is not useful, omit it.

## Decision Page Shape

Use `wiki/decisions/YYYY-MM-DD-short-decision.md`.

```markdown
# Decision: Short Decision

## Decision

State the decision plainly.

## Context

Why this came up.

## Rationale

Why this option was chosen.

## Tradeoffs

What this makes easier or harder.

## Sources

- `sources/...`

Last Updated: YYYY-MM-DD
```

## Review Output

After an ingest pass, report:

- pages created
- pages updated
- actions added or updated in `actions.md`
- source files processed
- uncertain claims
- new or suspicious names, acronyms, systems, projects, or jargon needing confirmation
- suggested next cleanup

Also include a consolidated handover section titled `Needs Confirmation`.

`Needs Confirmation` should be pleasant to scan and useful as a one-by-one clarification queue for the user. It should include only:

- all current needs-confirmation items from the ingest review, including suspicious names, acronyms, systems, projects, jargon, ambiguous owners, and uncertain high-impact claims.

Do not include the full `Open` action register in this handover by default. `actions.md` remains the canonical action register, but many captured TODOs may belong to other people or teams and should not be presented as Brian's assignment unless the user explicitly asks.

Format `Needs Confirmation` as grouped checklists by durable area when possible, such as `Team Operations`, `SMP Platform`, `Japan Interconnector`, and `Alerting and Ops`.

For each item, include:

- the exact term or claim to confirm;
- why confirmation is needed;
- the source path or related wiki page.

Keep `Needs Confirmation` concise enough to work through manually. Exclude items that are merely low-priority, already resolved, or not currently worth asking the user about.

</INSTRUCTIONS>

# AGENTS.md

This file provides guidance to AI coding agents working under `/Users/qn5792/repos/smp`.

## Overview

`/smp` is a parent directory, not a monorepo. It contains separate repositories for the **SMP (Scraper Management Platform)** and adjacent APAC scraper work. Read the repo-local `AGENTS.md` before changing files inside any child repo.

## Current Repos

| Repo | Purpose | Tooling |
|---|---|---|
| `scraper-scripts` | Raw standalone scraper scripts and behavioral references before Airflow adaptation | Python 3.13, `uv` |
| `smp-india` | India-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-japan` | Japan-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-tool` | Airflow Docker image, Helm values, Kubernetes scripts, Okta auth, Minikube/EKS setup | Python 3.12, `uv`, Helm, Kubernetes |
| `smp-dashboard` | Grafana dashboards and `cdh-dataset-manager` for CommonDataHub datasets | Python 3.11, `uv` |
| `apac-tsdb-scraper` | Poetry/solaris-sfw TSDB scrapers deployed through the older Lambda/tag flow | Python 3.12, Poetry |

Legacy names still appear in older docs and code comments:

- `scraper-dags` is now split into `smp-india` and `smp-japan`.
- `scraper-tool` is now `smp-tool`.
- `scraper-dashboard` is now `smp-dashboard`.

## End-to-End SMP Flow

```text
scraper-scripts  ->  smp-india / smp-japan  ->  smp-tool  ->  smp-dashboard
(reference impl)     (Airflow DAGs)             (runtime)     (CDH + Grafana)
```

Use `scraper-scripts` as the behavioral source when porting or validating a DAG. Region-specific DAG implementation now belongs in either `smp-india` or `smp-japan`; shared Airflow/platform behavior should come from `smp-common` or `smp-tool`, not be copied between region repos.

## Shared Rules

- Do not commit credentials, tokens, kubeconfigs, generated local secrets, or one-off path rewrites.
- Private dependencies come from ENGIE Artifactory. Prefer fixing local credentials over changing package indexes.
- Keep region boundaries explicit: India DAG code in `smp-india`, Japan DAG code in `smp-japan`.
- For Airflow DAGs, keep heavy imports out of module scope because Airflow parses DAG files frequently.
- For dashboard work, verify the upstream parquet/S3/CDH shape before changing Grafana JSON or dataset definitions.
- For infra changes, call out whether the change affects image build, Helm values, auth, DAG sync, runtime secrets, or Kubernetes namespaces.

## Cross-Repo Workflow: Porting A Script To A DAG

1. Run and observe the source script in `scraper-scripts`; capture inputs, outputs, credentials, file names, date logic, and failure behavior.
2. Choose the DAG repo by market ownership: `smp-india` or `smp-japan`.
3. Check whether an existing DAG for the same source should be extended before creating a new DAG.
4. Implement under `dags/src/`, keeping task runtime scraper modules under `dags/src/scripts/`.
5. Validate locally with `ruff`, `pytest`, and, when practical, `airflow dags test`.
6. Coordinate deployment through the appropriate branch/environment and `smp-tool` git-sync/Helm configuration.
7. Update `smp-dashboard` only after the DAG output is stable and visible where CDH/Grafana expects it.

## Branch And Environment Notes

- GitHub repos commonly use `main`; some DAG deployment flows still reference `dev`, `qa`, `master`, or region-specific branches.
- `smp-tool` has region-aware config under `config/japan/<env>/` and `config/india/<env>/`.
- Airflow namespaces are region-specific in current scripts, for example `smp-dev`, `smp-qa`, `smp-prod`, `smp-india-dev`, `smp-india-qa`, and `smp-india-prod`.
- Treat any old `scraper-dags`/ADO git-sync reference as potentially stale and verify against current `smp-tool` config before editing deployment behavior.

```text
Airflow worker
→ smp-india-prod-serviceaccount
→ arn:aws:iam::154989746575:role/dms-smp-india-prod-role
→ arn:aws:iam::046847230914:role/cdh_smpcdhindiaprod_53443
→ cdh-indiaassetsgeneration-337381 in ap-south-1
```

So the bucket and final CDH role may be in the same account, but the complete workload chain still begins in the SMP/EKS account. That’s worth recording because it’s exactly where trust failures could occur.

I wouldn’t preemptively investigate every possible policy, KMS key, proxy, endpoint, or CDH attachment. Run the probe and let the actual failure determine the next move:

- `AssumeRole` denied → trust-chain problem.
- `ListBucket` denied → role/bucket policy.
- `GetObject` denied → object permission.
- `kms:Decrypt` denied → KMS policy/grant.
- Timeout/DNS/proxy error → network path.
- Success → go for SCR-1209.

The existing production `NO_PROXY` already includes generic `*.s3.amazonaws.com` coverage, so I wouldn’t change it unless the real probe disproves it.

My vote: ad hoc in the prod worker first. If that’s politically or technically awkward, add a one-task, manually triggered `scr_1218_khaba_access_probe` DAG with no schedule and no write operations. Keep the probe script tiny enough that it can later become SCR-1209’s connectivity preflight—or simply be deleted after the evidence is captured. That feels like the least bullshit while still genuinely proving production access.
