# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/repos/smp

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# AGENTS.md

This file provides guidance to AI coding agents working under `/Users/qn5792/repos/smp`.

## Overview

`/smp` is a parent directory, not a monorepo. It contains separate repositories for the **SMP (Scraper Management Platform)** and adjacent APAC scraper work. Read the repo-local `AGENTS.md` before changing files inside any child repo.

## Current Repos

| Repo | Purpose | Tooling |
|---|---|---|
| `scraper-scripts` | Raw standalone scraper scripts and behavioral references before Airflow adaptation | Python 3.13, `uv` |
| `smp-india` | India-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-japan` | Japan-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-tool` | Airflow Docker image, Helm values, Kubernetes scripts, Okta auth, Minikube/EKS setup | Python 3.12, `uv`, Helm, Kubernetes |
| `smp-dashboard` | Grafana dashboards and `cdh-dataset-manager` for CommonDataHub datasets | Python 3.11, `uv` |
| `apac-tsdb-scraper` | Poetry/solaris-sfw TSDB scrapers deployed through the older Lambda/tag flow | Python 3.12, Poetry |

Legacy names still appear in older docs and code comments:

- `scraper-dags` is now split into `smp-india` and `smp-japan`.
- `scraper-tool` is now `smp-tool`.
- `scraper-dashboard` is now `smp-dashboard`.

## End-to-End SMP Flow

```text
scraper-scripts  ->  smp-india / smp-japan  ->  smp-tool  ->  smp-dashboard
(reference impl)     (Airflow DAGs)             (runtime)     (CDH + Grafana)
```

Use `scraper-scripts` as the behavioral source when porting or validating a DAG. Region-specific DAG implementation now belongs in either `smp-india` or `smp-japan`; shared Airflow/platform behavior should come from `smp-common` or `smp-tool`, not be copied between region repos.

## Shared Rules

- Do not commit credentials, tokens, kubeconfigs, generated local secrets, or one-off path rewrites.
- Private dependencies come from ENGIE Artifactory. Prefer fixing local credentials over changing package indexes.
- Keep region boundaries explicit: India DAG code in `smp-india`, Japan DAG code in `smp-japan`.
- For Airflow DAGs, keep heavy imports out of module scope because Airflow parses DAG files frequently.
- For dashboard work, verify the upstream parquet/S3/CDH shape before changing Grafana JSON or dataset definitions.
- For infra changes, call out whether the change affects image build, Helm values, auth, DAG sync, runtime secrets, or Kubernetes namespaces.

## Cross-Repo Workflow: Porting A Script To A DAG

1. Run and observe the source script in `scraper-scripts`; capture inputs, outputs, credentials, file names, date logic, and failure behavior.
2. Choose the DAG repo by market ownership: `smp-india` or `smp-japan`.
3. Check whether an existing DAG for the same source should be extended before creating a new DAG.
4. Implement under `dags/src/`, keeping task runtime scraper modules under `dags/src/scripts/`.
5. Validate locally with `ruff`, `pytest`, and, when practical, `airflow dags test`.
6. Coordinate deployment through the appropriate branch/environment and `smp-tool` git-sync/Helm configuration.
7. Update `smp-dashboard` only after the DAG output is stable and visible where CDH/Grafana expects it.

## Branch And Environment Notes

- GitHub repos commonly use `main`; some DAG deployment flows still reference `dev`, `qa`, `master`, or region-specific branches.
- `smp-tool` has region-aware config under `config/japan/<env>/` and `config/india/<env>/`.
- Airflow namespaces are region-specific in current scripts, for example `smp-dev`, `smp-qa`, `smp-prod`, `smp-india-dev`, `smp-india-qa`, and `smp-india-prod`.
- Treat any old `scraper-dags`/ADO git-sync reference as potentially stale and verify against current `smp-tool` config before editing deployment behavior.

The runbook actually retained the correct distinction while the code contradicted it, so this was a direct schedule regression.

Why we missed `YA000010SW`:

- The shared parser assumed a successful payload and immediately accessed `table.rows`.
- Weekly tests only constructed successful payloads and malformed row coverage; none supplied OCCTO’s application-error envelope.
- Daily handling learned about `YA000010SW` on June 23, but that logic was implemented only inside `daily.py`, not shared with weekly.
- The schedule test merely asserted the cron string. It didn’t assert the meaningful contract: “revised publication must run after the revised publication window.”
- The June 25 schedule change was committed directly without a PR review, so nobody caught the code/runbook contradiction.

So there were two review gaps: we tested structure instead of the publication-time behavior, and OCCTO error-envelope handling remained siloed in the daily scraper.

# AGENTS.md

This file provides guidance to AI coding agents working under `/Users/qn5792/repos/smp`.

## Overview

`/smp` is a parent directory, not a monorepo. It contains separate repositories for the **SMP (Scraper Management Platform)** and adjacent APAC scraper work. Read the repo-local `AGENTS.md` before changing files inside any child repo.

## Current Repos

| Repo | Purpose | Tooling |
|---|---|---|
| `scraper-scripts` | Raw standalone scraper scripts and behavioral references before Airflow adaptation | Python 3.13, `uv` |
| `smp-india` | India-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-japan` | Japan-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-tool` | Airflow Docker image, Helm values, Kubernetes scripts, Okta auth, Minikube/EKS setup | Python 3.12, `uv`, Helm, Kubernetes |
| `smp-dashboard` | Grafana dashboards and `cdh-dataset-manager` for CommonDataHub datasets | Python 3.11, `uv` |
| `apac-tsdb-scraper` | Poetry/solaris-sfw TSDB scrapers deployed through the older Lambda/tag flow | Python 3.12, Poetry |

Legacy names still appear in older docs and code comments:

- `scraper-dags` is now split into `smp-india` and `smp-japan`.
- `scraper-tool` is now `smp-tool`.
- `scraper-dashboard` is now `smp-dashboard`.

## End-to-End SMP Flow

```text
scraper-scripts  ->  smp-india / smp-japan  ->  smp-tool  ->  smp-dashboard
(reference impl)     (Airflow DAGs)             (runtime)     (CDH + Grafana)
```

Use `scraper-scripts` as the behavioral source when porting or validating a DAG. Region-specific DAG implementation now belongs in either `smp-india` or `smp-japan`; shared Airflow/platform behavior should come from `smp-common` or `smp-tool`, not be copied between region repos.

## Shared Rules

- Do not commit credentials, tokens, kubeconfigs, generated local secrets, or one-off path rewrites.
- Private dependencies come from ENGIE Artifactory. Prefer fixing local credentials over changing package indexes.
- Keep region boundaries explicit: India DAG code in `smp-india`, Japan DAG code in `smp-japan`.
- For Airflow DAGs, keep heavy imports out of module scope because Airflow parses DAG files frequently.
- For dashboard work, verify the upstream parquet/S3/CDH shape before changing Grafana JSON or dataset definitions.
- For infra changes, call out whether the change affects image build, Helm values, auth, DAG sync, runtime secrets, or Kubernetes namespaces.

## Cross-Repo Workflow: Porting A Script To A DAG

1. Run and observe the source script in `scraper-scripts`; capture inputs, outputs, credentials, file names, date logic, and failure behavior.
2. Choose the DAG repo by market ownership: `smp-india` or `smp-japan`.
3. Check whether an existing DAG for the same source should be extended before creating a new DAG.
4. Implement under `dags/src/`, keeping task runtime scraper modules under `dags/src/scripts/`.
5. Validate locally with `ruff`, `pytest`, and, when practical, `airflow dags test`.
6. Coordinate deployment through the appropriate branch/environment and `smp-tool` git-sync/Helm configuration.
7. Update `smp-dashboard` only after the DAG output is stable and visible where CDH/Grafana expects it.

## Branch And Environment Notes

- GitHub repos commonly use `main`; some DAG deployment flows still reference `dev`, `qa`, `master`, or region-specific branches.
- `smp-tool` has region-aware config under `config/japan/<env>/` and `config/india/<env>/`.
- Airflow namespaces are region-specific in current scripts, for example `smp-dev`, `smp-qa`, `smp-prod`, `smp-india-dev`, `smp-india-qa`, and `smp-india-prod`.
- Treat any old `scraper-dags`/ADO git-sync reference as potentially stale and verify against current `smp-tool` config before editing deployment behavior.

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T07:44:06.943Z

Yep. The clean solution is a manually triggered GitHub Actions workflow called **Promote release**.

End-of-sprint experience:

1. Open **Actions → Promote release → Run workflow**
2. Enter `Sprint 26`
3. Click **Run workflow**
4. Automation handles the rest

GitHub provides this UI automatically through `workflow_dispatch`; no custom interface is needed. [GitHub documentation](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/how-tos/manage-workflow-runs/manually-run-a-workflow)

The workflow should:

- Lock releases so two cannot run simultaneously.
- Validate the release name and generate `sprint-26`.
- Capture the exact `dev` commit being released.
- Confirm CI is green.
- Open and auto-merge `dev → qa`.
- Confirm QA CI passes.
- Open and auto-merge `qa → prod`.
- Confirm production CI passes.
- Create the annotated tag on the resulting `prod` commit.
- Optionally create a GitHub Release with generated notes.
- Produce a tidy summary with links to both PRs, CI, the tag, and release.

I’d make it respect the repository’s PR-only rule instead of using the administrator bypass we encountered today. Auto-merge waits for required checks before completing. [GitHub auto-merge documentation](https://docs.github.com/en/pull-requests/how-tos/merge-and-close-pull-requests/automatically-merging-a-pull-request)

One important implementation detail: the workflow should authenticate with a small GitHub App/bot credential. GitHub’s built-in workflow token generally suppresses new workflow runs caused by its own changes, which would interfere with CI on the generated promotion PRs. [GitHub token behavior](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/concepts/security/github_token)

My recommended safety level is “one click, then one production approval”:

```text
Enter “Sprint 26”
        ↓
CI → QA promotion → QA CI
        ↓
Approve production
        ↓
Prod promotion → Prod CI → sprint-26 tag → GitHub Release
```

GitHub Environments provide that approval button and can prevent the person who started the release from approving it themselves if desired. [GitHub environments documentation](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/deployments-and-environments)

I can build this in `smp-japan`; the only external setup would be installing/providing the GitHub App credential and optionally configuring the production approval group.

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T07:54:57.643Z

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T07:55:46.207Z

Yes—this should be an org-level release capability.

You already have the right homes:

- [`qrm-dms/sff-actions`](https://github.com/qrm-dms/sff-actions): implementation of the reusable promotion workflow
- [`qrm-dms/.github`](https://github.com/qrm-dms/.github): installable workflow template for adopting it in repositories

The shape would be:

```text
Repository button
      │
      ▼
Tiny local workflow
      │
      ▼
qrm-dms/sff-actions reusable workflow
      │
      ├─ validate source + release name
      ├─ dev → qa PR
      ├─ wait for QA checks
      ├─ production approval
      ├─ qa → prod PR
      ├─ wait for production checks
      └─ tag + GitHub Release
```

Each participating repo only needs a small caller file:

```yaml
name: Promote release

on:
  workflow_dispatch:
    inputs:
      release_name:
        description: Release name, e.g. Sprint 26
        required: true
        type: string

jobs:
  promote:
    uses: qrm-dms/sff-actions/.github/workflows/promote-release.yml@v1
    with:
      release_name: ${{ inputs.release_name }}
      source_branch: dev
      qa_branch: qa
      production_branch: prod
    secrets: inherit
```

The central workflow owns all behavior and safeguards. Repository-specific differences become inputs:

- Branch names
- Tag format
- Whether QA exists
- Production environment name
- Required checks
- GitHub Release creation
- Release-note generation
- Optional Jira/Slack notification
- Optional smoke-test workflow

The `.github` repo would expose that caller as an organization workflow template, so adopting it becomes **Actions → New workflow → Standard sprint promotion → Configure**. GitHub supports central organization templates through the `.github/workflow-templates` directory. [GitHub documentation](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/how-tos/reuse-automations/create-workflow-templates)

A few design decisions I’d recommend:

- Call the reusable workflow `promote-release.yml`.
- Publish it under a stable major version such as `@v1`.
- Use PRs and auto-merge—never branch-rule bypasses.
- Use an organization GitHub App for promotion authentication.
- Put the bot credentials in organization-level secrets, restricted to participating repositories.
- Add a per-repository `production` environment for approval.
- Use one organization-wide naming convention: human name `Sprint 26`, tag `sprint-26`.
- Fail on duplicate tags, dirty/diverged branch relationships, unsuccessful checks, or movement of `dev` during promotion.
- Use a concurrency lock so only one release can run per repository.
- Tag the verified `prod` merge commit—not the original `dev` commit.

Reusable workflows are specifically designed for this pattern, including organization sharing and caller-repository context. [GitHub reusable workflow documentation](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/reusing-workflow-configurations)

My recommendation: build the first `v1` in `sff-actions`, pilot it in `smp-japan`, then roll it into `smp-india` and other `dev → qa → prod` repositories through the `.github` template. That gives you one maintained pipeline and a tiny drop-in adapter per repo.

### User - 2026-07-30T07:57:34.829Z

Interesting. If rare case that a project don't conform to the dev,qa, prod.....??

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T07:57:59.607Z

Exactly—and we shouldn’t force every project into `dev → qa → prod`.

Standardize the release contract, not the branch topology.

I’d support a few explicit promotion profiles:

| Profile | Example | Flow |
|---|---|---|
| `three-stage` | SMP repos | `dev → qa → prod → tag` |
| `two-stage` | Smaller services | `develop → main → tag` |
| `tag-only` | Trunk-based libraries | Validate `main → tag → release` |
| `custom` | Exceptional repos | Repo supplies its own promotion steps but reuses central validation/tagging |

Branch names remain configurable:

```yaml
jobs:
  promote:
    uses: qrm-dms/sff-actions/.github/workflows/promote-release.yml@v1
    with:
      profile: three-stage
      source_branch: develop
      qa_branch: staging
      production_branch: main
      release_name: ${{ inputs.release_name }}
```

A smaller project could use:

```yaml
with:
  profile: two-stage
  source_branch: develop
  production_branch: main
  release_name: ${{ inputs.release_name }}
```

And a trunk-based library:

```yaml
with:
  profile: tag-only
  production_branch: main
  release_name: ${{ inputs.release_name }}
```

Internally, I wouldn’t build one enormous workflow full of conditionals. A cleaner structure is:

```text
sff-actions
├── promote-three-stage.yml
├── promote-two-stage.yml
├── release-tag-only.yml
└── shared release tooling
    ├── validate release
    ├── create/merge promotion PR
    ├── wait for checks
    ├── normalize tag
    └── create GitHub Release
```

That gives us:

- Opinionated defaults for normal projects
- Different branch names without custom code
- A supported path for simpler topologies
- Shared safety checks everywhere
- Escape hatches without turning the central pipeline into spaghetti

The invariant across every profile should be:

> A named, verified commit reaches the declared production branch, passes its required checks, and only then receives an immutable release tag.

So `dev`, `qa`, and `prod` are merely the SMP profile—not the organization-wide definition of a release.

### User - 2026-07-30T08:00:29.894Z

hmmmmm. What about keeping it simple yet flexible.. So instead of describing the topography of the branches, we only ask 2: release_branch and release_name...

regardless of the state of the reelease_branch, we simply create a tag off that...??

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T08:00:59.297Z

Yes—I think that’s the cleaner abstraction.

The central workflow doesn’t promote branches at all. Each repository decides how code reaches its release branch. The shared pipeline only cuts a release from an explicitly selected branch.

Inputs:

```text
release_branch: prod
release_name: Sprint 25
```

Result:

```text
prod HEAD
   ↓
verify exact commit
   ↓
annotated tag: sprint-25
   ↓
GitHub Release: Sprint 25
```

The key distinction:

> We don’t care about the branch topology or how the commit arrived—but we should care whether that exact commit is healthy.

So “regardless of branch state” should mean:

- No assumptions about `dev`, `qa`, `main`, or merge history
- No comparison with other branches
- No promotion performed
- Tag whatever commit is currently at `release_branch`

But retain minimal safety:

- Branch must exist.
- Capture its exact SHA at workflow start.
- Required CI for that exact SHA must be green.
- Release name must produce a valid, unused tag.
- Confirm the branch hasn’t moved before tagging.
- Create an annotated tag and GitHub Release.
- Never overwrite or move an existing tag.

The drop-in workflow becomes tiny:

```yaml
name: Cut release

on:
  workflow_dispatch:
    inputs:
      release_branch:
        description: Branch to release
        required: true
        default: prod
        type: string
      release_name:
        description: Release name, e.g. Sprint 25
        required: true
        type: string

jobs:
  release:
    uses: qrm-dms/sff-actions/.github/workflows/cut-release.yml@v1
    with:
      release_branch: ${{ inputs.release_branch }}
      release_name: ${{ inputs.release_name }}
    secrets: inherit
```

This creates a very clean responsibility boundary:

```text
Repository-specific process              Organization standard
───────────────────────────              ─────────────────────
How code reaches prod                    How a release is cut
Which environments exist                 Naming and tagging
Whether promotion uses PRs               CI verification
How deployment happens                   Immutability checks
                                         GitHub Release creation
```

For `smp-japan`, today’s sequence would be:

1. Promote using its existing `dev → qa → prod` process.
2. Run **Cut release** with `prod` and `Sprint 25`.

Later, promotion itself could be automated separately without coupling it to the organization-wide release mechanism.

I’d call the shared capability **Cut Release**, not **Promote Release**. That accurately describes its topology-agnostic job and keeps the contract pleasantly small.

# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/repos/smp

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# AGENTS.md

This file provides guidance to AI coding agents working under `/Users/qn5792/repos/smp`.

## Overview

`/smp` is a parent directory, not a monorepo. It contains separate repositories for the **SMP (Scraper Management Platform)** and adjacent APAC scraper work. Read the repo-local `AGENTS.md` before changing files inside any child repo.

## Current Repos

| Repo | Purpose | Tooling |
|---|---|---|
| `scraper-scripts` | Raw standalone scraper scripts and behavioral references before Airflow adaptation | Python 3.13, `uv` |
| `smp-india` | India-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-japan` | Japan-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-tool` | Airflow Docker image, Helm values, Kubernetes scripts, Okta auth, Minikube/EKS setup | Python 3.12, `uv`, Helm, Kubernetes |
| `smp-dashboard` | Grafana dashboards and `cdh-dataset-manager` for CommonDataHub datasets | Python 3.11, `uv` |
| `apac-tsdb-scraper` | Poetry/solaris-sfw TSDB scrapers deployed through the older Lambda/tag flow | Python 3.12, Poetry |

Legacy names still appear in older docs and code comments:

- `scraper-dags` is now split into `smp-india` and `smp-japan`.
- `scraper-tool` is now `smp-tool`.
- `scraper-dashboard` is now `smp-dashboard`.

## End-to-End SMP Flow

```text
scraper-scripts  ->  smp-india / smp-japan  ->  smp-tool  ->  smp-dashboard
(reference impl)     (Airflow DAGs)             (runtime)     (CDH + Grafana)
```

Use `scraper-scripts` as the behavioral source when porting or validating a DAG. Region-specific DAG implementation now belongs in either `smp-india` or `smp-japan`; shared Airflow/platform behavior should come from `smp-common` or `smp-tool`, not be copied between region repos.

## Shared Rules

- Do not commit credentials, tokens, kubeconfigs, generated local secrets, or one-off path rewrites.
- Private dependencies come from ENGIE Artifactory. Prefer fixing local credentials over changing package indexes.
- Keep region boundaries explicit: India DAG code in `smp-india`, Japan DAG code in `smp-japan`.
- For Airflow DAGs, keep heavy imports out of module scope because Airflow parses DAG files frequently.
- For dashboard work, verify the upstream parquet/S3/CDH shape before changing Grafana JSON or dataset definitions.
- For infra changes, call out whether the change affects image build, Helm values, auth, DAG sync, runtime secrets, or Kubernetes namespaces.

## Cross-Repo Workflow: Porting A Script To A DAG

1. Run and observe the source script in `scraper-scripts`; capture inputs, outputs, credentials, file names, date logic, and failure behavior.
2. Choose the DAG repo by market ownership: `smp-india` or `smp-japan`.
3. Check whether an existing DAG for the same source should be extended before creating a new DAG.
4. Implement under `dags/src/`, keeping task runtime scraper modules under `dags/src/scripts/`.
5. Validate locally with `ruff`, `pytest`, and, when practical, `airflow dags test`.
6. Coordinate deployment through the appropriate branch/environment and `smp-tool` git-sync/Helm configuration.
7. Update `smp-dashboard` only after the DAG output is stable and visible where CDH/Grafana expects it.

## Branch And Environment Notes

- GitHub repos commonly use `main`; some DAG deployment flows still reference `dev`, `qa`, `master`, or region-specific branches.
- `smp-tool` has region-aware config under `config/japan/<env>/` and `config/india/<env>/`.
- Airflow namespaces are region-specific in current scripts, for example `smp-dev`, `smp-qa`, `smp-prod`, `smp-india-dev`, `smp-india-qa`, and `smp-india-prod`.
- Treat any old `scraper-dags`/ADO git-sync reference as potentially stale and verify against current `smp-tool` config before editing deployment behavior.

### User - 2026-07-30T07:53:06.700Z

Option B: In-place update of airflow_values.yaml (recommended)
After push (to ECR), use a targeted Python one-liner to update only the digest: line in the values file. Because you're not round-tripping the whole YAML (just replacing one line), the Helm template expressions embedded in apiServerConfig and keda.query are completely safe.
 
Shell
python3 -c "
import re, sys
content = open('$VALUES_FILE').read()
content = re.sub(
    r'(digest:\s*)sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}',
    r'\g<1>$PUSHED_DIGEST',
    content
)
open('$VALUES_FILE', 'w').write(content)
"
 
 
Pros:
 
Single source of truth — airflow_values.yaml always reflects the last pushed image
Validator continues to work correctly (validates the real, current digest)
helm_upgrade_install.sh needs zero changes
Python is already used throughout the project
The file change is a clean, reviewable git commit
Cons:
 
Modifies a tracked file — in CI, the file needs a commit-back step (but that's normal GitOps practice and you'd want to track "which digest is deployed" anyway)
 
 
Option D: ECR query at deploy time (no file at all)
helm_upgrade_install.sh queries ECR for the digest of images.airflow.tag just before the helm call, then passes it via --set-string. No file management.
PHP
# Inside helm_upgrade_install.sh, derive repo name from the values file tag/repository
LIVE_DIGEST=$(aws ecr describe-images \
  --repository-name "<repo>" \
  --image-ids imageTag="<current-tag>" \
  --query 'imageDetails[0].imageDigest' --output text)
image_args+=(--set-string "images.airflow.digest=$LIVE_DIGEST")
 
Pros: Always uses live ECR state, no stale data ever, no file management.
 
Cons: AWS credentials are required at deploy time for the ECR query (they're already needed for EKS, so this is usually fine). Validator still validates the stale YAML digest. Adds an ECR API call to every deploy.
The core problem: images.airflow.digest in airflow_values.yaml becomes stale after every push and currently requires manual editing.
Option A (original plan): Digest file + --set-string override
After push → write config/<market>/<stage>/image_digest.txt → helm_upgrade_install.sh reads it and passes --set-string images.airflow.digest=<digest>.
 
Problems with this:
 
Two sources of truth: the YAML holds a stale digest, the file holds the current one
validate_airflow_helm_values.py runs before helm and validates only the YAML — so it validates a stale value
If someone runs helm_upgrade_install.sh after a newer push has changed the tag but the file digest is from an older push, you get a tag/digest mismatch at runtime
 
 
Option B: see message above
 
 
Option C: Helm values overlay file
After push → generate a small config/<market>/<stage>/image.yaml with just the images.airflow block → helm_upgrade_install.sh adds -f "$CONFIG_DIR/image.yaml" when it exists (second -f wins in Helm).
 
Pros: Clean separation, Helm-native, no large YAML manipulation.
 
Cons: Validator still validates the stale YAML digest. Two files tracking the same thing.
 
 
Option D: see message above


-- wdyt?

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T07:53:12.700Z

I’m leaning strongly toward Option B. I’ll quickly check the actual push, validation, and Helm scripts to see whether there are any repo-specific traps—especially multiple digest fields, CI commit-back behavior, or tag/digest coupling.

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T07:54:15.784Z

Option B is the right design for this repo. It keeps the deployed image reference reviewable, reproducible, and compatible with validation. I’d reject A and C because they create competing sources of truth, and D because deployment would depend on mutable external state rather than the reviewed Git state.

I’d make two adjustments:

- Don’t use the proposed regex exactly as written—it could silently replace multiple `digest:` fields in the future.
- Make the update fail unless exactly one digest was replaced. Otherwise a changed/malformed values file could produce a successful push without updating Git.

For example:

```bash
VALUES_FILE="$VALUES_FILE" PUSHED_DIGEST="$PUSHED_DIGEST" python3 - <<'PY'
import os
import re
from pathlib import Path

path = Path(os.environ["VALUES_FILE"])
digest = os.environ["PUSHED_DIGEST"]

if not re.fullmatch(r"sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}", digest):
    raise SystemExit(f"Invalid pushed digest: {digest!r}")

content = path.read_text()
updated, count = re.subn(
    r"(?m)^(\s{4}digest:\s*)sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}\s*$",
    rf"\g<1>{digest}",
    content,
)

if count != 1:
    raise SystemExit(f"Expected to update exactly one Airflow digest, updated {count}")

path.write_text(updated)
print(f"Updated {path} to {digest}")
PY
```

A small checked-in updater script with tests would be better than embedding this in CI, but the mechanism is sound.

One important workflow point: for CI, I’d use:

1. Build and push an immutable tag, preferably commit SHA/build ID.
2. Capture the digest for that exact pushed image.
3. Update the appropriate `config/<market>/<stage>/airflow_values.yaml`.
4. Open an automated PR or commit through the approved GitOps path.
5. Deploy from the resulting commit.

That avoids commit-loop and concurrent-build problems. If two builds finish out of order, direct commit-back could make an older build become the recorded deployment.

Also, the current repo passes `AIRFLOW_IMAGE_TAG` during Helm deployment, but every non-local values file has a digest, and the chart explicitly says the digest takes precedence. Therefore that tag override does not select the newly built image while the old digest remains. Updating the digest is not merely cleanup—it fixes the effective deployment reference.

My ranking: **B > D > C > A**, with B implemented as a guarded, atomic updater and preferably an automated PR rather than an unrestricted CI commit-back.

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T10:41:24.132Z

The key distinction is that Artifactory is only the distribution channel for `smp-common`; its source repo itself uses public PyPI. I’m checking whether we can replace that one package with an authenticated GitHub or sibling-checkout source without accidentally making the runtime image depend on a developer’s filesystem.

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T10:42:12.252Z

The cleanest workaround is to install `smp-common` directly from its GitHub repository in the DAG repos, while keeping Artifactory publishing for the Airflow runtime.

Both DAG repos currently pin `smp-common==0.6.0` and resolve it through Tools Artifactory ([India](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/smp-india/pyproject.toml:7), [Japan](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/smp-japan/pyproject.toml:7)). Add this instead:

```toml
[tool.uv.sources]
smp-common = {
    git = "https://github.com/qrm-dms/smp-common.git",
    tag = "v0.6.0",
}
```

Then:

1. Remove the `smp-core-dev` index from each DAG repo if nothing else uses it.
2. Regenerate and commit both `uv.lock` files.
3. Update the release process so each `smp-common` version has a matching immutable Git tag.
4. Let `smp-tool` and the runtime image continue installing the released wheel from Artifactory using CI/service credentials.

This works because `smp-common` itself resolves its dependencies from public PyPI; Artifactory is only its downstream distribution channel ([configuration](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/smp-common/pyproject.toml:7), [repo guidance](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/smp-common/AGENTS.md:41)). `uv.lock` also records the exact Git commit behind the tag, preserving reproducibility.

For active development on `smp-common`, contributors can temporarily use a sibling checkout:

```toml
[tool.uv.sources]
smp-common = { path = "../smp-common", editable = true }
```

That local path should never be committed.

One caveat: this removes the **Tools Artifactory** requirement for `smp-common`, but the DAG repos also obtain `engie-tsdb`, `dac-tools`, and potentially other packages through GEM Artifactory. If Mateo has no Artifactory access whatsoever, the proper wider solution is an approved prebuilt development container from CI—not shared credentials, copied wheels, or duplicated `smp-common` code.

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T14:05:09.337Z

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T14:06:20.238Z

Feasible — high confidence, small backend fix.

[DM-894](https://tractebel-et.atlassian.net/browse/DM-894) points to [PR-14963](https://tractebel-et.atlassian.net/browse/PR-14963). Jira access works.

Root cause:

- The front end correctly requests an upload URL, then sends the customized Sankey JSON using HTTP `PUT`.
- The backend’s internal-deployment path generates an S3 URL signed for `get_object`.
- Using that GET-signed URL for a PUT produces the reported `SignatureDoesNotMatch`.
- Production is configured as an “internal deployment,” despite using a public endpoint, so it takes this faulty path.

Recommended fix:

- Add a dedicated signed-upload URL generator using S3 `put_object`.
- In `generate_outputfile_get_signed_url`, select the PUT signer when `is_upload=True`; retain the existing GET behavior otherwise.
- Add tests verifying the S3 operation, bucket, key, and expiry.
- No front-end change should be required. S3 CORS already permits PUT.

This likely regressed during the February 2026 internal-deployment/S3 work. [PR-13136](https://tractebel-et.atlassian.net/browse/PR-13136) is useful historical context for the custom Sankey JSON and cache-bypass design, but it is not a duplicate.

Sizing: roughly 0.5–1 developer day including tests and validation in a non-production environment. Risk is low and localized. I found no existing PR or branch for DM-894/PR-14963.

I cloned `prosumer-app` (`dev`) and `prosumer-backend` (`prosumer_dev`) into the task’s `work/repos` directory. `/repos` itself is read-only on this machine. No Jira or repository changes were made.

### User - 2026-07-30T14:07:45.742Z

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T14:07:51.253Z

I’ll trace the three relevant pieces separately: the long-standing upload call, the later internal-S3 signer change, and when production began taking that internal path. That should distinguish the triggering commit from code that was merely already vulnerable.

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T14:14:23.240Z

Yep. Git history shows a latent design mistake that became an actual production regression after the deployment architecture changed.

### Timeline

1. **October 2024 — latent mismatch introduced**

[85d0a4a – PR-13136 custom Sankey upload fix](https://dev.azure.com/AA-SSO-EMEAI/Prosumer/_git/prosumer-backend/commit/85d0a4a2802e3710a263190a680b0c6a76ce02ea) added `is_upload`, but still returned:

```python
return scenario, generate_signed_get_url(key, ttl)
```

[35eecbc](https://dev.azure.com/AA-SSO-EMEAI/Prosumer/_git/prosumer-backend/commit/35eecbcc086c186405c11e240226bce6ee5ca07f) then explicitly routed the custom Sankey upload through it:

```python
generate_outputfile_get_signed_url(
    scenario,
    key=output_key,
    is_upload=True,
)
```

So the upload path knowingly used a helper named `generate_signed_get_url`. That looked odd, but worked with the old CloudFront implementation: its signature covered the URL and expiry, while the distribution allowed all HTTP methods.

2. **February 2026 — helper becomes genuinely GET-specific**

[615cf6c – “chore: fix certifi path”](https://dev.azure.com/AA-SSO-EMEAI/Prosumer/_git/prosumer-backend/commit/615cf6c6466687e20ca40a5a1223f2f5248e56e9) added special handling for internal deployments:

```python
if INTERNAL_DEPLOYMENT:
    return s3_client.generate_presigned_url(
        "get_object",
        ...
    )
```

This is the critical behavioral change. S3 SigV4 signs the HTTP operation, so a URL signed for `get_object` cannot be used by the frontend’s `PUT`.

The commit title is especially unhelpful because the change was much broader than fixing a certificate path.

3. **April 2026 — production activates the broken branch**

[53449a9 – production environment update](https://dev.azure.com/AA-SSO-EMEAI/Prosumer/_git/prosumer-backend/commit/53449a9aa8b863eaf7fa5fe142e33924a180cf4b) configured production with:

```bash
export INTERNAL_DEPLOYMENT=true
```

The comment even notes that this is misleading because production still uses a public ALB.

Both commits were included in [54e56e3 – “Deploy to prd”](https://dev.azure.com/AA-SSO-EMEAI/Prosumer/_git/prosumer-backend/commit/54e56e3d032a55618676df8a60c35c9a63666108), merged April 21, 2026.

From then on, the request became:

```text
Frontend PUT
  → results_json?action=upload
  → generate_outputfile_get_signed_url(is_upload=True)
  → generate_signed_get_url()
  → S3 presigns get_object
  → PUT using GET signature
  → SignatureDoesNotMatch
```

### Why tests missed it

The 2024 upload test mocks `generate_signed_get_url` and asserts that it was called. It never verifies whether the resulting URL is signed for `PUT`.

So the test effectively cemented the questionable abstraction:

```python
@patch("...generate_signed_get_url")
def test_generate_output_get_signed_url_is_upload_output(...):
    ...
    mock_signer.assert_called_with(custom_key, ANY)
```

There was no integration-style test combining:

```text
is_upload=True + INTERNAL_DEPLOYMENT=True + expected put_object
```

### Verdict

The exact regression trigger is **615cf6c**, activated in production by **53449a9 / the April 21 deployment**.

The older 2024 Sankey code contained the latent problem, but CloudFront happened to tolerate it. Switching internal deployments to method-specific S3 presigned URLs exposed it. The clean fix is to give the upload route a real `put_object` signer and add a regression test covering the complete internal-upload path.

### Assistant - 2026-07-30T14:17:18.646Z

Evidence is unusually direct:

- The failing URL is an S3 SigV4 URL.
- AWS reports `SignatureDoesNotMatch`.
- The frontend uses that URL with `PUT`.
- The backend signs it as `get_object` when `INTERNAL_DEPLOYMENT=true`.
- Production sets that flag to `true`.

- Generate or log the backend signer operation: it should currently show `get_object`.
- Locally presign the same key once for GET and once for PUT; PUT against the GET-signed URL should reproduce the same 403.

# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Baraluga Mind Agent Instructions

You are maintaining a local markdown second brain.

Your job is to turn raw captures into durable, useful wiki pages without inventing facts.

## Core Rules

- Read any nested `AGENTS.md` files that apply to the files being processed or edited.
- Preserve source material during ingest.
- Do not delete raw inputs unless explicitly asked.
- After processing an `inbox/` file, move it to the appropriate `sources/` folder when it remains useful as evidence.
- Leave an `inbox/` file in place when it still needs follow-up, review, or clarification.
- Do not fabricate details, links, names, dates, or decisions.
- Distinguish facts from inferences.
- Prefer small updates to existing wiki pages over creating duplicate notes.
- Link related wiki pages using Obsidian-style links like `[[concept-name]]`.
- Keep prose direct, dense, and useful.
- Use `UNCERTAIN:` when a claim needs verification.
- If source material is ambiguous, say so in the page.

## Actions

`actions.md` is the canonical register for follow-up work.

- Put every captured follow-up, task, owner commitment, or call to action in `actions.md`.
- Keep `actions.md` organized into exactly these top-level sections: `Today`, `Open`, `Waiting`, and `Done`.
- Add newly captured actions to `Open` by default.
- Use `Today` only when the user explicitly selects or prioritizes work for today.
- Use `Waiting` for actions blocked on another person, external answer, access, or event.
- Do not bury active TODOs inside wiki pages.
- Wiki pages may preserve context for an action, but the actionable checkbox belongs in `actions.md`.
- When adding an action, include a `Context` link to the related wiki page and a `Source` path to the captured evidence.
- Do not duplicate an existing open action; update it only when the new source adds material context.
- Move completed actions to `Done` instead of deleting them.
- Reconcile existing actions against new evidence during ingest. Move completed, superseded, or no-longer-relevant actions to `Done` with a brief source-backed reason; do not infer closure from ambiguous evidence.
- Source summaries may include non-canonical `Next Steps` when reflecting meeting content, but active tracking still belongs in `actions.md`.

## File Naming

Use lowercase kebab-case for all new files and directories.

Good:

- `wiki/concepts/second-brain.md`
- `wiki/projects/baraluga-mind.md`
- `wiki/decisions/2026-07-04-use-markdown-wiki.md`
- `inbox/2026-07-04-team-sync.md`

Avoid:

- `Second Brain.md`
- `second_brain.md`
- `Team Sync.md`
- `2026_07_04_notes.md`

## Process Improvement

Do not edit `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, or nested instruction files during normal ingest unless explicitly asked.

If you notice repeated friction, ambiguity, bad output, a useful convention, or instruction bloat, append a short note to `process-notes.md` instead.

Use this format:

```markdown
### YYYY-MM-DD

- Observation: ...
- Suggested rule: ...
- Example: ...
```

Instruction files should stay small and scoped. Prefer one local, source-specific instruction over expanding the root file when a rule applies only to one folder or content type.

## Ingest Behavior

When processing files from `inbox/` or `sources/`:

1. Identify the source type: article, meeting, voice note, PDF, pasted note, or mixed capture.
2. Extract durable entities:
   - people
   - projects
   - concepts
   - decisions
   - open questions
   - action items
3. Update existing pages when possible.
4. Create new pages only when the topic is likely to matter again.
5. Add or update captured action items in `actions.md`.
6. Add a short `Sources` section with the source filename or path.
7. Move processed `inbox/` files to the appropriate `sources/` subfolder when they remain useful as evidence:
   - `sources/articles/` for articles, links, and reading notes
   - `sources/meetings/` for meeting transcripts, agendas, and summaries
   - `sources/voice/` for voice-note transcripts and dictated thoughts
   - `sources/pdfs/` for PDFs and extracted PDF notes
   - `sources/notes/` for manual or AI-maintained daily notes
   - Leave the current Asia/Manila day's live daily note in `inbox/` unless the user explicitly asks to close or ingest it.
   - For a live `inbox/YYYY-MM-DD.md` daily note, reconcile its provisional task states with `actions.md`, then preserve it in `sources/notes/`.
   - When a daily note duplicates content in a Codex conversation export, prefer the structured daily note and use the transcript as backup evidence.
8. Add a short `Last Updated` date using `YYYY-MM-DD`.
9. Validate the complete ingest diff, then commit and push all ingest changes using an unscoped conventional commit.

## Wiki Page Shape

Use this structure when creating a new durable page:

```markdown
# Page Title

## Summary

One to three paragraphs explaining the durable point of the page.

## Details

Concrete facts, context, links, tradeoffs, and examples.

## Open Questions

- UNCERTAIN: ...

## Sources

- `sources/...`

Last Updated: YYYY-MM-DD
```

If a section is not useful, omit it.

## Decision Page Shape

Use `wiki/decisions/YYYY-MM-DD-short-decision.md`.

```markdown
# Decision: Short Decision

## Decision

State the decision plainly.

## Context

Why this came up.

## Rationale

Why this option was chosen.

## Tradeoffs

What this makes easier or harder.

## Sources

- `sources/...`

Last Updated: YYYY-MM-DD
```

## Review Output

After an ingest pass, report:

- pages created
- pages updated
- actions added or updated in `actions.md`
- source files processed
- uncertain claims
- new or suspicious names, acronyms, systems, projects, or jargon needing confirmation
- suggested next cleanup

Also include a consolidated handover section titled `Needs Confirmation`.

`Needs Confirmation` should be pleasant to scan and useful as a one-by-one clarification queue for the user. It should include only:

- all current needs-confirmation items from the ingest review, including suspicious names, acronyms, systems, projects, jargon, ambiguous owners, and uncertain high-impact claims.

Do not include the full `Open` action register in this handover by default. `actions.md` remains the canonical action register, but many captured TODOs may belong to other people or teams and should not be presented as Brian's assignment unless the user explicitly asks.

Format `Needs Confirmation` as grouped checklists by durable area when possible, such as `Team Operations`, `SMP Platform`, `Japan Interconnector`, and `Alerting and Ops`.

For each item, include:

- the exact term or claim to confirm;
- why confirmation is needed;
- the source path or related wiki page.

Keep `Needs Confirmation` concise enough to work through manually. Exclude items that are merely low-priority, already resolved, or not currently worth asking the user about.

</INSTRUCTIONS>
